\|d(F_{*}\theta)\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\beta-1}(\frac{3}{4}\mathbb{B}^{n};\wedge^{2}T^{*}\mathbb{R}^{n})}=\Big\|\sum_{i=1}^{n}d(\tilde{\rho}_{i}d\tilde{\phi}^{i})\Big\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\beta-1}(\frac{3}{4}\mathbb{B}^{n};\wedge^{2}T^{*}\mathbb{R}^{n})}\leq\sum_{i=1}^{n}\|d(\tilde{\rho}_{i}d\tilde{\phi}^{i})\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\beta-1}\left(\mathbb{R}^{n};\wedge^{2}T^{*}\mathbb{R}^{n}\right)}.

By Lemma
2.2
we have
\|\tilde{\rho}_{i}\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\beta}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}\lesssim_{\beta}\|\chi\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\beta}(\mathbb{B}^{n})}\|\rho_{i}\circ\Phi\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\beta}(\mathbb{B}^{n})}
and
\|\tilde{\phi}^{i}\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\alpha+1}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}\lesssim_{\alpha}\|\chi\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\alpha+1}(\mathbb{B}^{n})}\|\Phi\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\alpha+1}(\mathbb{B}^{n})}
, by (
4.15
) we have
\|\rho_{i}\circ\Phi\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\beta}(\mathbb{B}^{n})}\lesssim\|\rho_{i}\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\beta}(\mathbb{B}^{n})}
and
\|\Phi\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\alpha+1}(\mathbb{B}^{n};\mathbb{R}^{n})}\lesssim 1
. Combining them we get

\|\tilde{\rho}_{i}\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\beta}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}\lesssim_{\alpha,\beta}\|\rho_{i}\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\beta}(\mathbb{B}^{n})},\quad\|\tilde{\phi}^{i}\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\alpha+1}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}\lesssim_{\alpha}1,\quad 1\leq i\leq n.

(4.26)

Thus, to obtain (
4.24
) and complete the proof, it
suffices to show

\|d(\tilde{\rho}_{i}d\tilde{\phi}^{i})\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\beta-1}\left(\mathbb{R}^{n};\wedge^{2}T^{*}\mathbb{R}^{n}\right)}\lesssim_{\alpha,\beta}\|\tilde{\rho}_{i}\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\beta}(\mathbb{B}^{n})},\quad 1\leq i\leq n.

(4.27)

Similar to (
4.11
), we define 1-form
\tau_{i}
on
\mathbb{R}^{n}
by

\tau_{i}:=\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathfrak{P}(\tilde{\rho}_{i},d\tilde{\phi}^{i})-\mathfrak{P}(\tilde{\phi}^{i},d\tilde{\rho}_{i})+\mathfrak{R}(\tilde{\rho}_{i},d\tilde{\phi}^{i}),\quad 1\leq i\leq n.

By Lemma
4.9

(iv)
and
(v)
, we have
\tilde{\rho}_{i}d\tilde{\phi}^{i}=\mathfrak{P}(\tilde{\rho}_{i},d\tilde{\phi}^{i})+\mathfrak{P}(d\tilde{\phi}^{i},\tilde{\rho}_{i})+\mathfrak{R}(\tilde{\rho}_{i},d\tilde{\phi}^{i})
and
d\mathfrak{P}(\tilde{\phi}^{i},\tilde{\rho}_{i})=\mathfrak{P}(d\tilde{\phi}^{i},\tilde{\rho}_{i})+\mathfrak{P}(\tilde{\phi}^{i},d\tilde{\rho}_{i})
. Therefore for
1\leq i\leq n
,

d\tau_{i}=d\mathfrak{P}(\tilde{\rho}_{i},d\tilde{\phi}^{i})-d\mathfrak{P}(\tilde{\phi}^{i},d\tilde{\rho}_{i})+d\mathfrak{R}(\tilde{\rho}_{i},d\tilde{\phi}^{i})=d\mathfrak{P}(\tilde{\rho}_{i},d\tilde{\phi}^{i})+d\mathfrak{P}(d\tilde{\phi}^{i},\tilde{\rho}_{i})+d\mathfrak{R}(\tilde{\rho}_{i},d\tilde{\phi}^{i})=d(\tilde{\rho}_{i}d\tilde{\phi}^{i}),\quad\text{on }\mathbb{R}^{n}.

(4.28)

We claim

\|\tau_{i}\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\beta}(\mathbb{R}^{n};T^{*}\mathbb{R}^{n})}\lesssim\|\tilde{\rho}_{i}\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\beta}(\mathbb{R}^{n};T^{*}\mathbb{R}^{n})},\quad 1\leq i\leq n.

(4.29)

By Lemma
4.9

(i)
and
(iii)
, along with the fact that
\|\tilde{\phi}^{i}\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\alpha+1}}\lesssim 1
, we get
\|\mathfrak{P}(\tilde{\rho}_{i},d\tilde{\phi}^{i})\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\beta}}\lesssim\|\tilde{\rho}_{i}\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\beta}}
and
\|\mathfrak{R}(\tilde{\rho}_{i},d\tilde{\phi}^{i})\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\beta}}\lesssim\|\mathfrak{R}(\tilde{\rho}_{i},d\tilde{\phi}^{i})\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\alpha+\beta}}\lesssim\|\tilde{\rho}_{i}\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\beta}}
.

To complete the proof of (
4.29
), we
need to show

\|\mathfrak{P}(\tilde{\phi}^{i},d\tilde{\rho}_{i})\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\beta}(\mathbb{R}^{n};T^{*}\mathbb{R}^{n})}\lesssim_{\alpha,\beta}\|\tilde{\rho}_{i}\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\beta}(\mathbb{R}^{n};T^{*}\mathbb{R}^{n})},\quad 1\leq i\leq n.

(4.30)

We separate the proof of (
4.30
) into two cases:
\beta>1
and
0<\beta\leq 1
.

For the case
\beta>1
, (
4.30
) follows from Lemma
4.9

(i)
with (
4.26
) that
\tilde{\phi}^{i}\in\mathscr{C}^{\alpha+1}\subseteq\mathscr{C}^{\beta}
and
d\tilde{\rho}_{i}\in\mathscr{C}^{\beta-1}\subsetneq L^{\infty}
.
For the case
\beta\leq 1
, our assumption
0<\alpha\leq\beta
implies that
\alpha\in(0,1]
. So
\beta-\alpha-1<\beta-1\leq 0
and we have
d\tilde{\rho}_{i}\in\mathscr{C}^{\beta-1}\subsetneq\mathscr{C}^{\beta-\alpha-1}
. By Lemma
4.9

(ii)
along with
\tilde{\phi}^{i}\in\mathscr{C}^{\alpha+1}
from (
4.26
), we get
\mathfrak{P}(\tilde{\phi}^{i},d\tilde{\rho}_{i})\in\mathscr{C}^{(\alpha+1)+(\beta-\alpha-1)}=\mathscr{C}^{\beta}
, which gives (
4.30
) and establishes (
4.29
).

Using (
4.28
) and (
4.29
),
we see

\|d(\tilde{\rho}_{i}d\tilde{\phi}^{i})\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\beta-1}(\mathbb{R}^{n};\wedge^{2}T^{*}\mathbb{R}^{n})}=\|d\tau_{i}\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\beta-1}}\lesssim\|\tau_{i}\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\beta}}\lesssim\|\tilde{\rho}_{i}\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\beta}}
, establishing (
4.27
)
and completing the proof (by possibly increasing
C_{0}
).
∎

5
The Key Estimate

Let
\alpha>0
and
\beta\in[\alpha,\alpha+1]
. Suppose
\lambda^{1},\ldots,\lambda^{n}

are
\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}

1
-forms on an open set
U\subseteq\mathbb{R}^{n}
which span
the cotangent space at every point of
U
. If we know that
d\lambda^{j}\in\mathscr{C}^{\beta-1}_{\mathrm{loc}}
for each
j
, it is not necessarily true that
\lambda^{j}\in\mathscr{C}^{\beta}_{\mathrm{loc}}
.
However, it is a consequence of our main result (Theorem
3.1
) that near each point, one can always
change coordinates so that the forms are in
\mathscr{C}^{\beta}_{\mathrm{loc}}

(see also Corollary
5.17

and Remark
5.18
).

The next result is a special case of this idea,
where we present an intital
setting where we may find
a
\mathscr{C}^{\alpha+1}
-diffemorphism such that
F_{*}\lambda^{j}\in\mathscr{C}^{\beta}_{\mathrm{loc}}
.

Theorem 5.1
.

Let
\alpha>0
and
\beta\in[\alpha,\alpha+1]
. Let
x=(x^{1},\dots,x^{n})
and
y=(y^{1},\dots,y^{n})
be two coordinate systems for
\mathbb{R}^{n}
.
There exists
c=c(n,\alpha,\beta)>0
such that the following holds.

Suppose
\lambda^{i}
,
i=1,\dots,n\in\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}(\mathbb{B}^{n};T^{*}\mathbb{B}^{n})
are 1-forms on
\mathbb{B}^{n}
such that
\operatorname{supp}(\lambda^{i}-dx^{i})\subsetneq\frac{1}{2}\mathbb{B}^{n}
for each
i
, and

\sum_{i=1}^{n}(\|\lambda^{i}-dx^{i}\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}(\mathbb{B}^{n};T^{*}\mathbb{B}^{n})}+\|d\lambda^{i}\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\beta-1}(\mathbb{B}^{n};\wedge^{2}T^{*}\mathbb{B}^{n})})\leq c.

(5.1)

Then, there exists a
\mathscr{C}^{\alpha+1}
-diffeomorphism
F:\mathbb{B}^{n}_{x}\xrightarrow{\sim}\mathbb{B}^{n}_{y}
, such that
B^{n}(F(0),\frac{1}{6})\subseteq F(\frac{1}{3}\mathbb{B}^{n})\cap\frac{3}{4}\mathbb{B}^{n}
,
F_{*}\lambda^{i}\in\mathscr{C}^{\beta}(\mathbb{B}^{n};T^{*}\mathbb{B}^{n})
for
i=1,\dots,n
, and moreover

\displaystyle\|F-\mathrm{id}\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\alpha+1}(\mathbb{B}^{n};\mathbb{R}^{n})}+\sum_{i=1}^{n}\|F_{*}\lambda^{i}-dy^{i}\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\beta}\left(\mathbb{B}^{n};T^{*}\mathbb{B}^{n}\right)}

(5.2)

\displaystyle\leq c^{-1}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left(\|\lambda^{i}-dx^{i}\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}\left(\mathbb{B}^{n};T^{*}\mathbb{B}^{n}\right)}+\|d\lambda^{i}\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\beta-1}\left(\mathbb{B}^{n};\wedge^{2}T^{*}\mathbb{B}^{n}\right)}\right).

5.1
Outline of the proof: the dual Malgrange method

The proof of Theorem
5.1
is inspired by Malgrange’s proof
of the Newlander-Nirenberg Theorem
[
Mal69
]
.

Let
x=(x^{1},\dots,x^{n})
and
y=(y^{1},\dots,y^{n})
be two coordinate systems on the unit ball
\mathbb{B}^{n}\subset\mathbb{R}^{n}
. In this section, we write 1-forms
\lambda^{1},\dots,\lambda^{n}
,
\eta^{1},\dots,\eta^{n}
as

\lambda^{i}=dx^{i}+\sum_{j=1}^{n}a_{j}^{i}dx^{j},\quad\eta^{i}=dy^{i}+\sum_{j=1}^{n}b_{j}^{i}dy^{j},\quad i=1,\dots,n,

(5.3)

and define coefficient matrices

A:=(a_{j}^{i})_{n\times n}=\begin{pmatrix}a_{1}^{1}&\cdots&a_{n}^{1}\\
\vdots&\ddots&\vdots\\
a_{1}^{n}&\cdots&a_{n}^{n}\end{pmatrix},\quad B:=(b_{j}^{i})_{n\times n}=\begin{pmatrix}b_{1}^{1}&\cdots&b_{n}^{1}\\
\vdots&\ddots&\vdots\\
b_{1}^{n}&\cdots&b_{n}^{n}\end{pmatrix}.

(5.4)

In this section,
\lambda^{1},\ldots,\lambda^{n}
are given
\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}

1
-forms on
\mathbb{B}^{n}\subset\mathbb{R}^{n}
which span the cotangent space at every point.
And
\eta^{i}:=F_{*}\lambda^{i}
are the push-forward
1
-forms by the unknown

\mathscr{C}^{\alpha+1}
-diffeomorphism
F:\mathbb{B}^{n}\xrightarrow{\sim}\mathbb{B}^{n}
,
which we are solving for. Thus,
\eta^{1},\ldots,\eta^{n}
are also
\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}

1
-forms defined on
\mathbb{B}^{n}
which span the cotangent
space at every point.

As in Malgrange’s work
[
Mal69
]
,
the main idea is to choose
F
so that the matrix
B
satisfies a nonlinear
elliptic PDE. That
\eta=F_{*}\lambda\in\mathscr{C}^{\beta}
will follow
from the classical interior regularity of elliptic PDEs. We will show
such an
F
exists by showing that it suffices for
F
to satisfy
a different elliptic PDE, whose solution is guaranteed by
classical elliptic theory.

Given collections
(\lambda^{1},\dots,\lambda^{n})
and
(\eta^{1},\dots,\eta^{n})
of 1-forms on
\mathbb{B}^{1}
, as above, that both span their co-tangent spaces at every point, we define Riemannian metrics
g
and
h
by

\begin{split}&g=\sum_{i,j=1}^{n}g_{ij}dx^{i}dx^{j}:=\sum_{i,j,k=1}^{n}(\delta_{i}^{k}+a_{i}^{k})(\delta_{j}^{k}+a_{j}^{k})dx^{i}dx^{j},\\
&h=\sum_{i,j=1}^{n}h_{ij}dy^{i}dy^{j}:=\sum_{i,j,k=1}^{n}(\delta_{i}^{k}+b_{i}^{k})(\delta_{j}^{k}+b_{j}^{k})dy^{i}dy^{j}.\end{split}

(5.5)

Here,
\delta_{i}^{j}
,
\delta_{ij}
,
\delta^{ij}
are the Kronecker delta functions:

\delta_{i}^{j}=\delta_{ij}=\delta^{ij}=\begin{cases}1,&i=j,\\
0,&i\neq j.\end{cases}

(5.6)

We use the following notations from classical Riemannian differential geometry:

\begin{split}&g^{ij}:=g(dx^{i},dx^{j}),\quad\sqrt{\det g}:=\left|\frac{\lambda^{1}\wedge\dots\wedge\lambda^{n}}{dx^{1}\wedge\dots\wedge dx^{n}}\right|,\\
&h^{ij}:=h(dy^{i},dy^{j}),\quad\sqrt{\det h}:=\left|\frac{\eta^{1}\wedge\dots\wedge\eta^{n}}{dy^{1}\wedge\dots\wedge dy^{n}}\right|.\end{split}

(5.7)

Remark 5.2
.

(a)

We can write
h=\sum_{i=1}^{n}\eta^{i}\cdot\eta^{i}
. It is non-degenerate since
\eta^{1},\dots,\eta^{n}
span the cotangent space at every point. Moreover
\eta^{1},\dots,\eta^{n}
form an orthogonal basis with respect to this metric
h
. Similar remarks hold for
g=\sum_{i=1}^{n}\lambda^{i}\cdot\lambda^{i}
.

(b)

Using matrix notations in (
5.3
) we have
(h_{ij})_{n\times n}=(I+B)^{\top}(I+B)
and so we know
(h^{ij})_{n\times n}=(h_{ij})^{-1}=(I+B)^{-1}\left((I+B)^{-1}\right)^{\top}
and
\sqrt{\det h}=\det(I+B)
. Similarly, we have
(g_{ij})=(I+A)^{\top}(I+A)=(g^{ij})^{-1}
and
\sqrt{\det g}=\det(I+A)
. More importantly, we have the following lemma.

Lemma 5.3
.

Let
B
,
h^{ij}
and
\sqrt{\det h}
be as above. Then
h^{ij}
and
\sqrt{\det h}
are rational functions of the components of
B
. Moreover for every
\gamma>0
, there is a
c_{n,\gamma}>0
such that

h^{ij},\sqrt{\det h}:\left\{B\in\mathscr{C}^{\gamma}(\mathbb{B}^{n};\mathbb{M}^{n\times n}):\|B\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\gamma}}<c_{n,\gamma}\right\}\to\mathscr{C}^{\gamma}(\mathbb{B}^{n}),\quad 1\leq i,j\leq n,

are norm continuous maps, with

\sum_{i,j=1}^{n}\|h^{ij}-\delta^{ij}\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\gamma}(\mathbb{B}^{n})}\leq c_{n,\gamma}^{-1}\|B\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\gamma}(\mathbb{B}^{n};\mathbb{M}^{n\times n})},\quad\|\sqrt{\det h}-1\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\gamma}(\mathbb{B}^{n})}\leq c_{n,\gamma}^{-1}\|B\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\gamma}(\mathbb{B}^{n};\mathbb{M}^{n\times n})}.

Remark 5.4
.

The same results hold for
g^{ij}
and
\sqrt{\det g}
. Namely,
\|g^{ij}-\delta^{ij}\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}}+\|\sqrt{\det g}-1\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}}\leq c_{n,\alpha}^{-1}\|A\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}}
holds with the same constant
c_{n,\alpha}>0
.

Proof.

By Lemma
2.2
, the space
\mathscr{C}^{\gamma}(\mathbb{B}^{n};\mathbb{M}^{n\times n})
is closed under matrix multiplication. By Remark
5.2

(b)

\sqrt{\det h}=\det(I+B)
is a polynomial in the components of
B
, so in particular is a norm continuous function on
\mathscr{C}^{\gamma}(\mathbb{B}^{n};\mathbb{M}^{n\times n})
. Note that
\det(I+0)=1
so we have
\|\sqrt{\det h}-1\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\gamma}(\mathbb{B}^{n})}\lesssim_{\gamma}\|B\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\gamma}(\mathbb{B}^{n};\mathbb{M}^{n\times n})}
when the right hand side is small.

By Lemma
4.13
, by choosing
c_{n,\gamma}<\tilde{c}_{\mathbb{B}^{n},\gamma}
where
\tilde{c}_{\mathbb{B}^{n},\gamma}
is the constant in Lemma
4.13
, we see that the map
B\mapsto(I+B)^{-1}
is
\mathscr{C}^{\gamma}
-norm continuous with
\|(I+B)^{-1}-I\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\gamma}(\mathbb{B}^{n};\mathbb{M}^{n\times n})}\leq 2\|B\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\gamma}(\mathbb{B}^{n};\mathbb{M}^{n\times n})}
.

Thus, in the domain
\|B\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\gamma}}<c_{n,\gamma}
, the map
B\mapsto(I+B)^{-1}\left((I+B)^{-1}\right)^{\top}
is also continuous and satisfies
\|(I+B)^{-1}\left((I+B)^{-1}\right)^{\top}-I\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\gamma}}\lesssim\|(I+B)^{\top}(I+B)-I\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\gamma}}\lesssim\|B\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\gamma}}
. It follows with
(h^{ij})_{n\times n}=(h_{ij})^{-1}_{n\times n}=(I+B)^{-1}\left((I+B)^{-1}\right)^{\top}
, we have that
h^{ij}
are norm continuous and
\|h^{ij}-\delta^{ij}\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\gamma}}\lesssim\|B\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\gamma}}
.

By possibly shrinking
c_{n,\gamma}
we get
\|\sqrt{\det h}-1\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\gamma}}\leq c_{n,\gamma}^{-1}\|B\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\gamma}}
and
\sum_{i,j=1}^{n}\|h^{ij}-\delta^{ij}\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\gamma}}\leq c_{n,\gamma}^{-1}\|B\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\gamma}}
.
∎

Convention 5.5
.

Given a Riemannian metric
h
, we use the co-differential
\vartheta_{h}
as the adjoint of differential with respect to
h
. That is, for any
k
-form
\phi
and any compactly supported
(k-1)
-form
\psi
,

(\vartheta_{h}\phi,\psi)_{h}:=(\phi,d\psi)_{h}=\int h(\phi,d\psi)\ d\operatorname{vol_{h}},

where
d\operatorname{vol_{h}}
is the Riemannian volume density induced by
h
. In local coordinates,
d\operatorname{vol_{h}}=\sqrt{\det h}\ d\operatorname{vol_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}}
, where
d\operatorname{vol_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}}
is the usual Lebesgue density on
\mathbb{R}^{n}
.
We write
\vartheta_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}
for the usual co-differential with respect to the flat metric
\sum_{i,j=1}^{n}\delta_{ij}dy^{i}dy^{j}
on
\mathbb{R}^{n}
.

Lemma 5.6
.

Let
\alpha>0
, and let
\lambda^{1},\dots,\lambda^{n}
be
\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}
1-forms defined on
\mathbb{B}^{n}
that span the cotangent space at every point, with
(a_{i}^{j})
,
g
,
(g^{ij})
, and
\sqrt{\det g}
given in (
5.4
), (
5.5
), and (
5.7
).

Suppose
F=\mathrm{id}+R:\mathbb{B}^{n}_{x}\to\mathbb{B}^{n}_{y}
is a
\mathscr{C}^{\alpha+1}
-diffeomorphism that satisfies

\sum_{i,j=1}^{n}\frac{\partial}{\partial x^{j}}\Big(\sqrt{\det g}\cdot g^{ij}\frac{\partial R^{k}}{\partial x^{i}}\Big)=\sum_{i,j=1}^{n}\frac{\partial}{\partial x^{j}}\big(\sqrt{\det g}\cdot g^{ij}a_{i}^{k}\big),\quad\text{in }\mathbb{B}^{n}_{x},\quad k=1,\dots,n.

(5.8)

Then for the pushforward 1-forms
\eta^{k}=F_{*}\lambda^{k}
,
k=1,\dots,n
, the coefficients
(b_{i}^{j})
defined in (
5.4
) satisfy

\sum_{i,j=1}^{n}\frac{\partial}{\partial y^{i}}\left(\sqrt{\det h}h^{ij}b_{j}^{k}\right)=0,\quad\text{in }\mathbb{B}^{n}_{y},\quad k=1,\dots,n.

(5.9)

Proof.

Note that the composition of a
C^{1}
-function and a
\mathscr{C}^{\alpha+1}
-diffeomorphism is still
C^{1}
, and being compactly supported is preserved under homeomorphism, so we have

C_{c}^{1}(\mathbb{B}_{x}^{n})=\{v\circ F:v\in C_{c}^{1}(\mathbb{B}_{y}^{n})\}.

(5.10)

By assumption
R\in\mathscr{C}^{\alpha+1}(\mathbb{B}^{n};\mathbb{R}^{n})
, and
a_{i}^{k},g^{ij},\sqrt{\det g}\in\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}(\mathbb{B}^{n})
, so (
5.8
) makes sense in
\mathscr{C}^{\alpha-1}_{\mathrm{loc}}(\mathbb{B}^{n}_{x})\subsetneq C_{c}^{1}(\mathbb{B}^{n}_{x})^{\prime}
and the equality
can be viewed as elements of the dual of
C_{c}^{1}(\mathbb{B}^{n})
.

For any
u\in C_{c}^{1}(\mathbb{B}^{n})
, integrating by parts, we obtain

\displaystyle 0

\displaystyle=\left\langle\sum_{i,j=1}^{n}\frac{\partial}{\partial x^{j}}\left(\sqrt{\det g}\cdot g^{ij}\Big(\frac{\partial R^{k}}{\partial x^{i}}-a_{i}^{k}\Big)\right),u\right\rangle_{\mathbb{B}^{n}_{x}}=-\int_{\mathbb{B}^{n}}\sum_{i,j=1}^{n}g^{ij}\left(\frac{\partial R^{k}}{\partial x^{i}}-a_{i}^{k}\right)\frac{\partial u}{\partial x^{j}}\sqrt{\det g}dx

\displaystyle=-\langle dR^{k}-(\lambda^{k}-dx^{k}),du\rangle_{\mathbb{B}^{n}_{x};g}=-\langle dF^{k}-\lambda^{k},du\rangle_{\mathbb{B}_{x}^{n};g}

Here
\langle\cdot,\cdot\rangle_{\mathbb{B}_{x}^{n};g}
are the dual pairs for linear functionals and test functions induced by
g
. Namely, for
u,v\in C^{0}(\mathbb{B}^{n})
and
\phi,\psi\in C^{0}(\mathbb{B}^{n};T^{*}\mathbb{B}^{n})
,
\langle u,v\rangle_{\mathbb{B}^{n};g}=\int_{\mathbb{B}^{n}}uv\sqrt{\det g}dx
and
\langle\phi,\psi\rangle_{\mathbb{B}^{n};g}=\int_{\mathbb{B}^{n}}g(\phi,\psi)\sqrt{\det g}dx
.

Using (
5.8
), we get
\langle dF^{k}-\lambda^{k},du\rangle_{\mathbb{B}^{n}_{x};g}=0
for all
u\in C_{c}^{1}(\mathbb{B}^{n})
. By (
5.10
) we have
\langle dF^{k}-\lambda^{k},d(v\circ F)\rangle_{\mathbb{B}^{n}_{x};g}=0
for all
v\in C_{c}^{1}(\mathbb{B}^{n}_{y})
.

Note that
F_{*}(g(\phi,\psi))=(F_{*}g)(F_{*}\phi,F_{*}\psi)=h(F_{*}\phi,F_{*}\psi)
for all
\phi,\psi\in C^{0}_{\mathrm{loc}}(\mathbb{B}^{n};T^{*}\mathbb{B}^{n})
, and
F_{*}\sqrt{\det g}=\sqrt{\det F_{*}g}=\sqrt{\det h}
, so
we have, for every
v\in C_{c}^{1}(\mathbb{B}^{n}_{y})
,

0=\langle dF^{k}-\lambda^{k},d(v\circ F)\rangle_{\mathbb{B}^{n}_{x};g}=\langle F_{*}(dF^{k}-\lambda^{k}),F_{*}F^{*}dv\rangle_{\mathbb{B}^{n}_{y};h}=\langle dy^{k}-\eta^{k},dv\rangle_{\mathbb{B}^{n}_{y};h}=\int_{\mathbb{B}^{n}}\sum_{i,j=1}^{n}h^{ij}b_{j}^{k}\frac{\partial v}{\partial y^{i}}\sqrt{\det h}dy.

Integrating by parts, we obtain (
5.9
).
∎

We will choose a coordinate chart
F
so that (
5.8
)
is satisfied, and therefore (
5.9
)
will be satisfied as well.

To prove Theorem
5.1
we will
prove the following:

•

There exists a
R\in\mathscr{C}^{\alpha+1}(\mathbb{B}^{n};\mathbb{R}^{n})
satisfying (
5.8
) with boundary condition
R\big|_{\partial\mathbb{B}^{n}}=0
. Moreover,
we can choose
R
with
\|R\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\alpha+1}}\lesssim_{\alpha,\beta}\|A\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}}
.
Thus, by taking
c>0
small, we may take
\|R\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\alpha+1}}
small.

•

When
\|R\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\alpha+1}}
is small,
F=\mathrm{id}+R
is a
\mathscr{C}^{\alpha+1}
-diffeomorphism of
\mathbb{B}^{n}
.
And under the assumption
\operatorname{supp}A\subsetneq\frac{1}{2}\mathbb{B}^{n}
, we have
\|B\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\beta}(\partial\mathbb{B}^{n})}\lesssim_{\alpha,\beta}\|A\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}}
and
\|d\eta\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\beta-1}}\lesssim_{\alpha,\beta}\|d\lambda\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\beta-1}}
.
In particular, by taking
c>0
small, we may take

\|B\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\beta}(\partial\mathbb{B}^{n})}+\|d\eta\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\beta-1}}
small.

•

Using that
B\in\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}(\partial\mathbb{B}^{n};\mathbb{M}^{n\times n})
satisfies (
5.9
), if
\|B\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\beta}(\partial\mathbb{B}^{n})}+\|d\eta\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\beta-1}}
is small, we will show
B\in\mathscr{C}^{\beta}(\mathbb{B}^{n};\mathbb{M}^{n\times n})
and
\|B\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\beta}}\lesssim_{\alpha,\beta}\|A\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}}
.

The last step above requires the Zygmund-Hölder well-posedness for the Dirichlet problem.

Lemma 5.7

(The Dirichlet Problem)
.

Let
\gamma>0
, and let
U
be a bounded domain with smooth boundary. Then for
f\in\mathscr{C}^{\gamma-2}(U)
and
g\in\mathscr{C}^{\gamma}(\partial U)
there is a unique
u\in\mathscr{C}^{\gamma+2}(U)
such that
\bigtriangleup u=f
and
u\big|_{\partial U}=g
.

Moreover, the solution map
(f,g)\mapsto u
is continuous bilinear map
\mathscr{C}^{\gamma-2}(U)\times\mathscr{C}^{\gamma}(\partial U)\rightarrow\mathscr{C}^{\gamma}(U)
.

See
[
FR95
, Theorem 15]
for a proof of Lemma
5.7
.

Definition 5.8

(Dirichlet solution on ball)
.

Let
\gamma>0
and
f\in\mathscr{C}^{\gamma-2}(\mathbb{B}^{n})
, we write
\mathfrak{D}(f)
for the unique solution
u\in\mathscr{C}^{\gamma}(\mathbb{B}^{n})
such that
\bigtriangleup u=f
and
u\big|_{\partial\mathbb{B}^{n}}=0
.
For a
\mathscr{C}^{\gamma-2}
-vector valued function
g=(g_{1},\dots,g_{m})
on
\mathbb{B}^{n}
, we let
\mathfrak{D}(g):=(\mathfrak{D}(g_{1}),\dots,\mathfrak{D}(g_{m}))
.

Remark 5.9
.

In Lemma
5.7
, for
\gamma>0
,
\mathscr{C}^{\gamma}(\partial\mathbb{B}^{n})
is the Zymgund-Hölder space on the sphere
\mathbb{S}^{n-1}=\partial\mathbb{B}^{n}
. One can use Definition
4.2
to define
\mathscr{C}^{\gamma}
-functions on it.
Note that the sphere is a compact manifold, and therefore
\mathscr{C}^{\gamma}_{\mathrm{loc}}(\mathbb{S}^{n-1})=\mathscr{C}^{\gamma}(\mathbb{S}^{n-1})
.
The norm can be defined using any finite atlas,
and the equivalence class of the norm does not depend on
the choice of atlas. Moreover we have

\|f\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\gamma}(\partial\mathbb{B}^{n})}\approx_{\gamma}\inf\{\|\tilde{f}\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\gamma}(\mathbb{B}^{n})}:\tilde{f}\big|_{\partial\mathbb{B}^{n}}=f\},

(5.11)

since the trace operator
(\cdot)|_{\partial\mathbb{B}^{n}}:\mathscr{C}^{\gamma}(\mathbb{B}^{n})\to\mathscr{C}^{\gamma}(\partial\mathbb{B}^{n})
is continuous and surjective; see
[
Tri10
, Theorem 2.7.2]
.
In fact given a function
f\in\mathscr{C}^{\gamma}(\mathbb{S}^{n-1})
, we can take
\tilde{f}(x)=f(\frac{x}{|x|})\chi(|x|)
where
\chi\in C_{c}^{\infty}(-\frac{1}{2},2)
such that
\chi(1)=1
, then we have
\tilde{f}\big|_{\mathbb{S}^{n-1}}=f
and
\|\tilde{f}\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\gamma}(\mathbb{B}^{n})}\approx_{\gamma}\|f\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\gamma}(\mathbb{S}^{n-1})}
.

5.2
The existence proposition

In this section, we show that there exists a
\mathscr{C}^{\alpha+1}
-diffeomorphism
F=\mathrm{id}+R
solving (
5.8
) and which satisfies
good estimates.

Proposition 5.10
.

Let
\alpha>0
and let
\beta\in[\alpha,\alpha+1]
. There is a
c_{1}=c_{1}(n,\alpha,\beta)\in(0,1)
such that, if
A=(a_{j}^{k})_{n\times n}:\mathbb{B}^{n}\to\mathbb{M}^{n\times n}
satisfies

-

A\in\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}_{c}(\frac{1}{2}\mathbb{B}^{n};\mathbb{M}^{n\times n})
and
\|A\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}}<c_{1}
,

then the matrix
(I+A)(x)
is invertible for every
x\in\mathbb{B}^{n}
, and there is a
\mathscr{C}^{\alpha+1}
-map
F=\mathrm{id}+R
on
\mathbb{B}^{n}
such that

(i)

R
solves the equation

\sum_{i,j=1}^{n}\frac{\partial}{\partial x^{j}}\Big(\sqrt{\det g}\cdot g^{ij}\frac{\partial R^{k}}{\partial x^{i}}\Big)=\sum_{i,j=1}^{n}\frac{\partial}{\partial x^{j}}\big(\sqrt{\det g}\cdot g^{ij}a_{i}^{k}\big),\quad\text{in }\mathbb{B}^{n}_{x},\quad k=1,\dots,n.

(
5.8
)

with boundary condition
R\big|_{\partial\mathbb{B}^{n}}=0
, and we have

\textstyle\|R\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\alpha+1}(\mathbb{B}^{n};\mathbb{R}^{n})}+\|\nabla R\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\beta}(\mathbb{B}^{n}\backslash\frac{3}{4}\mathbb{B}^{n};\mathbb{R}^{n})}\leq c_{1}^{-1}\|A\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}}.

(5.12)

(ii)

F:\mathbb{B}^{n}_{x}\to\mathbb{B}^{n}_{y}
is a
\mathscr{C}^{\alpha+1}
-diffeomorphism such that
B^{n}(F(0),\frac{1}{6})\subseteq F(\frac{1}{3}\mathbb{B}^{n})\cap\frac{3}{4}\mathbb{B}^{n}
.

(iii)

Let
\Phi=F^{-1}:\mathbb{B}^{n}_{y}\to\mathbb{B}^{n}_{x}
be its
inverse map, then

\|\nabla\Phi-I\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}(\mathbb{B}^{n};\mathbb{M}^{n\times n})}+\|\nabla\Phi-I\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\beta}(\partial\mathbb{B}^{n};\mathbb{M}^{n\times n})}\leq c_{1}^{-1}\|A\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}}.

(5.13)

In particular
\|\Phi\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\alpha+1}(\mathbb{B}^{n};\mathbb{R}^{n})}\leq c_{1}^{-1}
.

Remark 5.11
.

The map
F
in Proposition
5.10
is uniquely determined by
A
. This is due to the well-posedness of the Dirichlet problem for the second order elliptic equations, since
R
satisfies (
5.8
) with
R\big|_{\partial\mathbb{B}^{n}}=0
.

Remark 5.12
.

As we will see in the proof of Theorem
5.1
, the map
F

from Proposition
5.10

is the map of the same name in Theorem
5.1
.

Proof.

We let
c_{1}
be a small constant which may change from line to line.
Note that if (
5.12
) and (
5.13
) are valid for some
\tilde{c}_{1}
, then they are also valid for any
0<c_{1}\leq\tilde{c}_{1}
.

First pick
c_{1}<\tilde{c}_{\mathbb{B}^{n},\alpha}
where
\tilde{c}_{\mathbb{B}^{n},\alpha}
is the constant in Lemma
4.13
. By Lemma
4.13
, the assumption
\|A\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}}<c_{1}(<\tilde{c}_{\mathbb{B}^{n},\alpha})
implies that
I+A
is invertible at every point and

(I+A)^{-1}\in\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}(\mathbb{B}^{n};\mathbb{M}^{n\times n})
. Therefore,
g
given in (
5.5
) is indeed a
\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}
-Riemannian metric.

By the assumption
\operatorname{supp}A\subsetneq\frac{1}{2}\mathbb{B}^{n}
, we have
\sqrt{\det g}\cdot g^{ij}\big|_{\mathbb{B}^{n}\backslash\frac{1}{2}\mathbb{B}^{n}}=\delta^{ij}
. Since
(\sqrt{\det g}g^{ij}(x))_{n\times n}
is an invertible matrix for
x\in\frac{1}{2}\mathbb{B}^{n}
, the second order operator
\sum_{i,j=1}^{n}\partial_{x^{j}}(\sqrt{\det g}\cdot g^{ij}\partial_{x^{i}})
is uniformly elliptic on
\mathbb{B}^{n}
.
Classical existence theorems (for example,
[
GT01
, Theorem 8.3]
) show that for each
k=1,\dots,n
there exists
5
5

5

Here
H^{1}(\mathbb{B}^{n})
stands for the classical
L^{2}
-Sobolev space of order
1
, and
H^{-1}(\mathbb{B}^{n})=H_{0}^{1}(\mathbb{B}^{n})^{*}
is the
L^{2}
-Sobolev space of order
-1
.
a
R^{k}\in H^{1}(\mathbb{B}^{n})
that satisfies (
5.8
) with Dirichlet boundary condition
R^{k}\big|_{\partial\mathbb{B}^{n}}=0
, since
\sum_{i,j=1}^{n}\frac{\partial}{\partial x^{j}}\left(\sqrt{\det g}g^{ij}a_{i}^{k}\right)\in\mathscr{C}^{\alpha-1}(\mathbb{B}^{n})\subset H^{-1}(\mathbb{B}^{n})
.
By a classical regularity estimate (see
[
GT01
, Theorem 8.34]
or
[
FR95
, Theorem 15]
), we know
R^{k}\in\mathscr{C}^{\alpha+1}(\mathbb{B}^{n};\mathbb{R}^{n})
.

To show
\|R\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\alpha+1}}\lesssim\|A\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}}
, we write (
5.8
) as

\bigtriangleup R^{k}=\sum_{i,j=1}^{n}\frac{\partial}{\partial x^{j}}\left(\left(\delta^{ij}-\sqrt{\det g}g^{ij}\right)\frac{\partial R^{k}}{\partial x^{i}}\right)+\sum_{i,j=1}^{n}\frac{\partial}{\partial x^{j}}\left(\sqrt{\det g}g^{ij}a_{i}^{k}\right),\quad\text{in }\mathbb{B}^{n}_{x},\quad k=1,\dots,n.

(5.14)

By Remark
5.4
(see also Lemma
5.3
), we see that
\|\delta^{ij}-\sqrt{\det g}g^{ij}\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}(\mathbb{B}^{n})}\lesssim_{\alpha}\|A\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}}
.
Therefore we have

\|\bigtriangleup R\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\alpha-1}}\lesssim_{\alpha}\sum_{i,j,k=1}^{n}\left(\|\delta^{ij}-\sqrt{\det g}g^{ij}\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}}\|\partial_{x^{i}}R^{k}\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}}+\|\sqrt{\det g}g^{ij}a_{i}^{k}\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}}\right)\lesssim\|A\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}}\|R\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\alpha+1}}+\|A\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}},

(5.15)

where the implicit constants depend only on
n
and
\alpha
but not
A
or
R
.

The assumption
R\big|_{\partial\mathbb{B}^{n}}=0
implies that
R=\mathfrak{D}(\bigtriangleup R)
, where
\mathfrak{D}
is the zero Dirichlet boundary solution operator given in Definition
5.8
. Since, by Lemma
5.7
,
\mathfrak{D}:\mathscr{C}^{\alpha-1}(\mathbb{B}^{n})\to\mathscr{C}^{\alpha+1}(\mathbb{B}^{n})
is bounded, (
5.15
) implies

\|R\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\alpha+1}(\mathbb{B}^{n};\mathbb{R}^{n})}\leq\tilde{C}_{1}\|A\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}(\mathbb{B}^{n};\mathbb{M}^{n\times n})}\|R\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\alpha+1}(\mathbb{B}^{n};\mathbb{R}^{n})}+\tilde{C}_{1}\|A\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}(\mathbb{B}^{n};\mathbb{M}^{n\times n})},

(5.16)

where
\tilde{C}_{1}=\tilde{C}_{1}(n,\alpha)>1
is a constant depending only on
n
and
\alpha
but not
A
or
R
.

Choosing
c_{1}
small enough so that
c_{1}\tilde{C}_{1}\leq\frac{1}{3}
, then we get
\|R\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\alpha+1}}\leq\frac{1}{3}\|R\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\alpha+1}}+\tilde{C}_{1}\|A\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}}
when
A
satisfies the assumption
\|A\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}}<c_{1}
. Therefore

\textstyle\|R\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\alpha+1}(\mathbb{B}^{n};\mathbb{R}^{n})}\leq\frac{3}{2}\tilde{C}_{1}\|A\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}}\leq\frac{1}{2}c_{1}^{-1}\|A\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}},\quad\text{when }\|A\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}}<c_{1}\leq(3\tilde{C}_{1})^{-1}.

(5.17)

This is part of the estimate in (
5.12
).

Next we show
\|R\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\beta+1}(\mathbb{B}^{n}\backslash\frac{3}{4}\mathbb{B}^{n})}\lesssim\|A\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}}
. Note that by the support assumption
\operatorname{supp}A\subsetneq\frac{1}{2}\mathbb{B}^{n}
we have
\sqrt{\det g}g^{ij}\big|_{\mathbb{B}^{n}\backslash\frac{1}{2}\mathbb{B}^{n}}=\delta^{ij}
and
a_{i}^{k}\big|_{\mathbb{B}^{n}\backslash\frac{1}{2}\mathbb{B}^{n}}=0
, so the right hand side of (
5.14
) is zero in
\mathbb{B}^{n}\backslash\frac{1}{2}\mathbb{B}^{n}
. Therefore each
R^{k}
are harmonic functions in the domain
\mathbb{B}^{n}\backslash\frac{1}{2}\mathbb{B}^{n}
.

The estimate
\|R\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\alpha+1}(\mathbb{B}^{n})}\lesssim_{\alpha}\|A\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}}
implies
\|R\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\alpha+1}(\partial(\frac{1}{2}\mathbb{B}^{n}))}\lesssim_{\alpha}\|A\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}}
since the trace map
\mathscr{C}^{\alpha+1}(\mathbb{B}^{n})\to\mathscr{C}^{\alpha+1}(\partial(\frac{1}{2}\mathbb{B}^{n}))
is bounded (see Remark
5.9
). By classical interior estimates of harmonic functions (for example,
[
GT01
, Theorem 2.10]
) since
\bigtriangleup R\big|_{\mathbb{B}^{n}\backslash\frac{1}{2}\mathbb{B}^{n}}=0
, we have

\|R\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\beta+1}(\partial\frac{3}{4}\mathbb{B}^{n})}\lesssim\|R\|_{C^{\lceil\beta\rceil+1}(\partial\frac{3}{4}\mathbb{B}^{n})}\lesssim\|R\|_{C^{0}(\mathbb{B}^{n}\backslash\frac{2}{3}\mathbb{B}^{n})}\lesssim\|A\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}}.

(5.18)

Therefore, along with the fact that
R\big|_{\partial\mathbb{B}^{n}}=0
,
R\big|_{\partial(\mathbb{B}^{n}\backslash\frac{3}{4}\mathbb{B}^{n})}=R\big|_{\partial\frac{3}{4}\mathbb{B}^{n}}\cup R\big|_{\partial\mathbb{B}^{n}}
has
\mathscr{C}^{\beta+1}
norm bounded by a constant times
\|A\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}}
. By classical regularity estimates of harmonic functions (also see Lemma
5.7
) on
\mathbb{B}^{n}\backslash\frac{3}{4}\mathbb{B}^{n}
we know

\|R\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\beta+1}(\mathbb{B}^{n}\backslash\frac{3}{4}\mathbb{B}^{n})}\lesssim_{\beta}\|R\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\beta+1}\big(\partial(\mathbb{B}^{n}\backslash\frac{3}{4}\mathbb{B}^{n})\big)}\lesssim_{\alpha,\beta}\|A\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}}.

In particular, there is a
\tilde{C}_{2}=\tilde{C}_{2}(n,\alpha,\beta)>0
that depends on neither
A
nor
R
such that

\|\nabla R\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\beta}(\mathbb{B}^{n}\backslash\frac{3}{4}\mathbb{B}^{n})}\leq\tilde{C}_{2}\|A\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}}.

(5.19)

Taking
c_{1}<\frac{1}{2}\tilde{C}_{2}^{-1}
we have
\|\nabla R\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\beta}(\mathbb{B}^{n}\backslash\frac{3}{4}\mathbb{B}^{n})}\leq\frac{1}{2}c_{1}^{-1}\|A\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}}
. Combining this with (
5.17
), completes the proof of (
5.12
).

We can take
c_{1}>0
possibly smaller so that
c_{1}<\frac{1}{3}(C_{0}\tilde{C}_{1})^{-1}
, where
C_{0}
is the constant in Proposition
4.10
and
\tilde{C}_{1}
is the constant in (
5.16
). By (
5.17
) we know
\|R\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\alpha+1}}\leq C_{0}^{-1}
. So by Proposition
4.10
, the map
F=\mathrm{id}+R
has
\mathscr{C}^{\alpha+1}
-inverse. We conclude
\Phi=F^{-1}\in\mathscr{C}^{\alpha+1}(\mathbb{B}^{n};\mathbb{R}^{n})
.

Since
\|R\|_{C^{1}(\mathbb{B}^{n};\mathbb{M}^{n\times n})}\lesssim_{\alpha}\|R\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\alpha+1}(\mathbb{B}^{n};\mathbb{R}^{n})}
, by possibly shrinking
c_{1}
we can ensure
\|R\|_{C^{0}}+\|\nabla R\|_{C^{0}}\leq\frac{1}{2}
. So
F(0)=R(0)\in B^{n}(0,\frac{1}{2})
, which implies
B^{n}(F(0),\frac{1}{6})\subseteq B^{n}(0,\frac{1}{2}+\frac{1}{6})\subset\frac{3}{4}\mathbb{B}^{n}
, and
|F(x_{1})-F(x_{2})|\geq|x_{1}-x_{2}|-|R(x_{1})-R(x_{2})|\geq\frac{1}{2}|x_{1}-x_{2}|
for
x_{1},x_{2}\in\mathbb{B}^{n}
.
Thus, if
|F(x)-F(0)|<\frac{1}{6}
then
|x-0|<\frac{1}{3}
; i.e.,
B^{n}(F(0),\frac{1}{6})\subseteq F(\frac{1}{3}\mathbb{B}^{n})
. So
B^{n}(F(0),\frac{1}{6})\subseteq F(\frac{1}{3}\mathbb{B}^{n})\cap\frac{3}{4}\mathbb{B}^{n}
finishing the proof of
(ii)
.

Finally we prove
(iii)
. Note that by Proposition
4.10

(i)
, (
5.13
) gives
\|\Phi\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\alpha+1}(\mathbb{B}^{n};\mathbb{R}^{n})}\lesssim 1
, which is
\|\Phi\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\alpha+1}}\leq c_{1}^{-1}
by choosing
c_{1}
small.

By Proposition
4.10

(ii)
and using that
c_{1}<\frac{1}{3}(C_{0}\tilde{C}_{1})^{-1}
, we get
\|\nabla\Phi-I\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}}\leq C_{0}\|R\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\alpha+1}}\leq C_{0}\tilde{C}_{1}\|A\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}}\leq\frac{1}{2}c_{1}^{-1}\|A\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}}
, which proves half of (
5.13
).

To show the second half of (
5.13
), we need to show
\|\nabla\Phi-I\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\beta}(\partial\mathbb{B}^{n})}\lesssim\|A\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}}
.

The assumption
R\big|_{\partial\mathbb{B}^{n}}=0
implies
F\big|_{\partial\mathbb{B}^{n}}=\mathrm{id}\big|_{\partial\mathbb{B}^{n}}=\Phi\big|_{\partial\mathbb{B}^{n}}
and therefore

(\nabla\Phi-I)\big|_{\partial\mathbb{B}^{n}}=((\nabla\Phi)\circ\Phi^{-1})\big|_{\partial\mathbb{B}^{n}}-I=(\nabla F)^{-1}\big|_{\partial\mathbb{B}^{n}}-I.

Fix
\chi\in C_{c}^{\infty}(2\mathbb{B}^{n}\backslash\frac{3}{4}\mathbb{B}^{n})
such that
\chi\equiv 1
in a neighborhood of
\partial\mathbb{B}^{n}
, so
\nabla R(x)=\chi(x)\nabla R(x)
for
x\in\mathbb{B}^{n}
near
\partial\mathbb{B}^{n}
.

We shrink
c_{1}>0
so that
c_{1}<\tilde{c}_{\mathbb{B}^{n},\beta}\cdot(\tilde{C}_{\mathbb{B}^{n},\beta}\cdot\tilde{C}_{2}\|\chi\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\beta}})^{-1}
, where
\tilde{C}_{\mathbb{B}^{n},\beta}
is in (
4.18
),
\tilde{C}_{2}
is in (
5.19
), and
\tilde{c}_{\mathbb{B}^{n},\beta}
is in Lemma
4.13
. Then the assumption
\|A\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}}<c_{1}
implies

\|\chi\nabla R\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\beta}(\mathbb{B}^{n})}\leq\tilde{C}_{\mathbb{B}^{n},\beta}\|\chi\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\beta}_{c}(\mathbb{B}^{n}\backslash\frac{3}{4}\mathbb{B}^{n})}\|\nabla R\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\beta}(\mathbb{B}^{n}\backslash\frac{3}{4}\mathbb{B}^{n})}\leq\tilde{C}_{\mathbb{B}^{n},\beta}\cdot\tilde{C}_{2}\|A\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}_{c}(\frac{1}{2}\mathbb{B}^{n})}<\tilde{c}_{\mathbb{B}^{n},\beta}.

Therefore we can apply Lemma
4.13
to
\chi\nabla R\in\mathscr{C}^{\beta}(\mathbb{B}^{n};\mathbb{M}^{n\times n})
to obtain
\|(I+\chi\nabla R)^{-1}-I\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\beta}}\leq 2\|\chi\nabla R\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\beta}}
. Hence, along with (
5.11
),

\|(I+\nabla R)^{-1}-I\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\beta}(\partial\mathbb{B}^{n})}\lesssim\|(I+\chi\nabla R)^{-1}-I\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\beta}(\mathbb{B}^{n})}\lesssim\|\chi\nabla R\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\beta}(\mathbb{B}^{n})}\lesssim_{\chi}\|R\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\beta+1}(\mathbb{B}^{n}\backslash\frac{3}{4}\mathbb{B}^{n})}\lesssim\|A\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}}.

So by possibly shrinking
c_{1}>0
, we get
\|\nabla\Phi-I\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\beta}(\partial\mathbb{B}^{n})}\leq\frac{1}{2}c_{1}^{-1}\|A\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}}
, which completes the second half of (
5.13
).
∎

We now have pushforward 1-forms
\eta^{1}=F_{*}\lambda^{1},\dots,\eta^{n}=F_{*}\lambda^{n}
. Their norms admit some control, as the next lemma shows.

Lemma 5.13
.

Let
\alpha>0
and let
\beta\in[\alpha,\alpha+1]
. There is a
c_{2}=c_{2}(n,\alpha,\beta)>0
such that the following holds.
Let
A\in\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}_{c}(\frac{1}{2}\mathbb{B}^{n},\mathbb{M}^{n\times n})
be the coefficient matrix for
\lambda^{1},\dots,\lambda^{n}
(see (
5.3
)) satisfying the assumptions of Proposition
5.10
and also satisfying

(a)

\|A\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}(\mathbb{B}^{n};\mathbb{M}^{n\times n})}<c_{2}
.

(b)

For
k=1,\dots,n
,
d\lambda^{k}\in\mathscr{C}^{\beta-1}\left(\mathbb{B}^{n};{\textstyle\bigwedge}^{2}T^{*}\mathbb{B}^{n}\right)
with
\sum_{k=1}^{n}\|d\lambda^{k}\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\beta-1}(\mathbb{B}^{n};\wedge^{2}T^{*}\mathbb{B}^{n})}<c_{2}
.

Suppose
\Phi=F^{-1}:\mathbb{B}^{n}_{y}\to\mathbb{B}^{n}_{x}
satisfies the conclusions of Proposition
5.10
. Then, for the 1-forms
\eta^{k}=\Phi^{*}\lambda^{k}
(
k=1,\dots,n
) with coefficient matrix
B=(b^{i}_{j})_{n\times n}
(see (
5.3
)), we have:

1.

B
satisfies the PDE system (
5.9
).

2.

\|B\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}(\mathbb{B}^{n};\mathbb{M}^{n\times n})}+\|B\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\beta}(\partial\mathbb{B}^{n};\mathbb{M}^{n\times n})}<c_{2}^{-1}\|A\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}}
.

3.

d\eta^{k}\in\mathscr{C}^{\beta-1}\left(\mathbb{B}^{n};{\textstyle\bigwedge}^{2}T^{*}\mathbb{B}^{n}\right)
for
k=1,\dots,n
, with
\|d\eta^{k}\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\beta-1}(\mathbb{B}^{n};\wedge^{2}T^{*}\mathbb{B}^{n})}<c_{2}^{-1}\|d\lambda^{k}\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\beta-1}}
.

Proof.

Part
1
is obtained in Lemma
5.6
.

For part
2
, write
\Phi=(\phi^{1},\dots,\phi^{n})
, where
\phi^{k}\in\mathscr{C}^{\alpha+1}(\mathbb{B}^{n})
,
k=1,\dots,n
. Therefore

\eta^{k}=\Phi^{*}\Big(dx^{k}+\sum_{i=1}^{n}a_{i}^{k}dx^{i}\Big)=d\phi^{k}+\sum_{i=1}^{n}(a_{i}^{k}\circ\Phi)d\phi^{i},\quad b_{j}^{k}=\frac{\partial(\phi^{k}-y^{k})}{\partial y^{j}}+\sum_{i=1}^{n}(a_{i}^{k}\circ\Phi)\frac{\partial\phi^{i}}{\partial y^{j}},\quad 1\leq j,k\leq n.

(5.20)

From (
5.20
) we know that
\|B\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}}\lesssim\|\nabla\Phi-I\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}}+\|A\circ\Phi\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}}\|\nabla\Phi\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}}
. By Proposition
5.10

(iii)
we know
\|\Phi\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\alpha+1}}\lesssim 1
and
\|\nabla\Phi-I\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}}\lesssim\|A\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}}
. By Proposition
4.10

(i)
we get
\|A\circ\Phi\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}}\lesssim\|A\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}}
. Combining these we get

\|B\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}}\lesssim\|\nabla\Phi-I\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}}+\|A\circ\Phi\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}}\|\nabla\Phi\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}}\lesssim\|A\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}}.

(5.21)

Since we have
A\equiv 0
outside
\frac{1}{2}\mathbb{B}^{n}
in particular
A\big|_{\partial\mathbb{B}^{n}}=0
, it follows that
\eta^{k}=d\phi^{k}
on
\partial\mathbb{B}^{n}
.
Therefore
\|B\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\beta}(\partial\mathbb{B}^{n})}=\|\nabla\Phi-I\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\beta}(\partial\mathbb{B}^{n})}
.
So by Proposition
5.10

(iii)

\|B\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\beta}(\partial\mathbb{B}^{n})}=\|\nabla\Phi-I\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\beta}(\partial\mathbb{B}^{n})}\lesssim\|A\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}}

(5.22)

By choosing
c_{2}>0
small, (
5.21
) and (
5.22
) complete the proof of
2
.

Finally, for
3
, we apply
Proposition
4.10

(iii)

with
\theta=\lambda^{k}
,
for each
k=1,\dots,n
. Since
d(F_{*}\theta)=d(F_{*}\lambda^{k})=d\eta^{k}
, by (
4.16
) we get
\|d\eta^{k}\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\beta-1}(\mathbb{B}^{n};\wedge^{2}T^{*}\mathbb{B}^{n})}\lesssim\|d\lambda^{k}\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\beta-1}}
. Taking
c_{2}
smaller, we complete the proof.
∎

5.3
The regularity proposition

In this part, we show that the 1-forms
\eta^{1},\dots,\eta^{n}
are indeed
\mathscr{C}^{\beta}
, by using the interior regularity theory for elliptic PDEs.

Proposition 5.14
.

Let
\alpha>0
and
\beta\in[\alpha,\alpha+1]
.
There is a
c_{3}=c_{3}(n,\alpha,\beta)>0
, such that if
\eta^{1},\dots,\eta^{n}\in\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}(\mathbb{B}^{n};T^{*}\mathbb{B}^{n})
with coefficient matrix
B\in\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}(\mathbb{B}^{n};\mathbb{M}^{n\times n})
(see (
5.3
)) such that
B
solves the PDE (
5.9
),
B\big|_{\partial\mathbb{B}^{n}}\in\mathscr{C}^{\beta}(\partial\mathbb{B}^{n};\mathbb{M}^{n\times n})
with

\|B\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}(\mathbb{B}^{n};\mathbb{M}^{n\times n})}+\|B\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\beta}(\partial\mathbb{B}^{n};\mathbb{M}^{n\times n})}+\sum_{l=1}^{n}\|d\eta^{l}\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\beta-1}\left(\mathbb{B}^{n};\wedge^{2}T^{*}\mathbb{B}^{n}\right)}<c_{3},

(5.23)

then
B\in\mathscr{C}^{\beta}(\mathbb{B}^{n};\mathbb{M}^{n\times n})
. Moreover

\|B\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\beta}(\mathbb{B}^{n};\mathbb{M}^{n\times n})}\leq c_{3}^{-1}\Big(\|B\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}(\mathbb{B}^{n};\mathbb{M}^{n\times n})}+\|B\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\beta}(\partial\mathbb{B}^{n};\mathbb{M}^{n\times n})}+\sum_{l=1}^{n}\|d\eta^{l}\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\beta-1}(\mathbb{B}^{n};\wedge^{2}T^{*}\mathbb{B}^{n})}\Big).

(5.24)

Proof.

We can write
B=\mathfrak{D}(\bigtriangleup B)+(B-\mathfrak{D}(\bigtriangleup B))
, where
\mathfrak{D}
is defined in Definition
5.8
and is the zero Dirichlet boundary solution operator to the Laplacian equation on the unit ball.

Note that
B-\mathfrak{D}(\bigtriangleup B)
is the harmonic function whose boundary value equals to
B\big|_{\partial\mathbb{B}^{n}}
(which might not be zero). By Lemma
5.7
using the assumption
B\big|_{\partial\mathbb{B}^{n}}\in\mathscr{C}^{\beta}(\partial\mathbb{B}^{n};\mathbb{M}^{n\times n})
, we get
B-\mathfrak{D}(\bigtriangleup B)\in\mathscr{C}^{\beta}(\mathbb{B}^{n};\mathbb{M}^{n\times n})
and

\|B-\mathfrak{D}(\bigtriangleup B)\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\beta}(\mathbb{B}^{n})}\lesssim\|B\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\beta}(\partial\mathbb{B}^{n})}.

(5.25)

We can rewrite (
5.9
) as

-\sum_{i=1}^{n}\frac{\partial}{\partial y^{i}}b_{i}^{k}=\sum_{i,j=1}^{n}\frac{\partial}{\partial y^{i}}\left(\big(\sqrt{\det h}h^{ij}-\delta^{ij}\big)b_{j}^{k}\right),\quad\text{in }\mathbb{B}^{n}_{y},\quad k=1,\dots,n.

(5.26)

The left hand side of (
5.26
) is
\vartheta_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\eta^{k}
. By Lemma
5.3
, the right hand side of (
5.26
) is the derivatives of rational functions of the components of
B
, which vanish to second order at
B=0
. More precisely, using Lemma
5.3
, we can rewrite (
5.26
) as

\vartheta_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\eta^{k}=-\sum_{i=1}^{n}\frac{\partial}{\partial y^{i}}b_{i}^{k}=\sum_{i=1}^{n}\frac{\partial}{\partial y^{i}}\mathcal{R}_{i}^{k}(B),\quad\text{in }\mathbb{B}^{n}_{y},\quad k=1,\dots,n.

(5.27)

Here
\mathcal{R}_{i}^{k}
are rational functions (see Lemma
5.3
) defined in a neighborhood of origin in
\mathbb{M}^{n\times n}
with
|\mathcal{R}_{i}^{k}(u)|\lesssim|u|_{\mathbb{M}^{n\times n}}^{2}
for suitably small matrices
u\in\mathbb{M}^{n\times n}
, and we have

|\mathcal{R}_{i}^{k}(u_{1})-\mathcal{R}_{i}^{k}(u_{2})|\lesssim(|u_{1}|_{\mathbb{M}^{n\times n}}+|u_{2}|_{\mathbb{M}^{n\times n}})|u_{1}-u_{2}|_{\mathbb{M}^{n\times n}},\quad\text{when }u_{1},u_{2}\in\mathbb{M}^{n\times n}\text{ small}.

(5.28)

We can pass this fact from matrices to matrix-valued functions. Indeed,
\mathcal{R}_{i}^{k}
has convergent power expansion in a neighborhood of
0
as

\mathcal{R}_{i}^{k}(u)=\sum_{r=2}^{\infty}\sum_{j_{1},\dots,j_{r},l_{1},\dots,l_{r}=1}^{n}a_{i,r;l_{1}\dots l_{r}}^{k;j_{1}\dots j_{r}}u_{j_{1}}^{l_{1}}\dots u_{j_{r}}^{l_{r}},\quad\text{converging when }|u|_{\mathbb{M}^{n\times n}}\text{ is small.}

(5.29)

Here
a_{i,r;l_{1}\dots l_{r}}^{k;j_{1}\dots j_{r}}\in\mathbb{R}
. The power expansion starts at
r=2
since the zero and the first order terms all vanish.

By Lemma
2.2
, we can replace
u\in\mathbb{M}^{n\times n}
in (
5.29
) by
f\in\mathscr{C}^{\gamma}(\mathbb{B}^{n};\mathbb{M}^{n\times n})
, and as in (
5.28
), for
\gamma>0
there is a
\tilde{C}_{\mathcal{R},\gamma}>0
, such that when
\|f_{1}\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\gamma}(\mathbb{B}^{n};\mathbb{M}^{n\times n})}+\|f_{2}\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\gamma}(\mathbb{B}^{n};\mathbb{M}^{n\times n})}\leq\tilde{C}_{\mathcal{R},\gamma}^{-1}
,

\|\mathcal{R}_{i}^{k}(f_{1})-\mathcal{R}_{i}^{k}(f_{2})\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\gamma}(\mathbb{B}^{n})}\leq\tilde{C}_{\mathcal{R},\gamma}(\|f_{1}\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\gamma}(\mathbb{B}^{n};\mathbb{M}^{n\times n})}+\|f_{2}\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\gamma}(\mathbb{B}^{n};\mathbb{M}^{n\times n})})\|f_{1}-f_{2}\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\gamma}(\mathbb{B}^{n};\mathbb{M}^{n\times n})},\quad 1\leq i,k\leq n.

(5.30)

Using the fact that
\bigtriangleup\eta^{k}=d\vartheta_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\eta^{k}+\vartheta_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}d\eta^{k}
, we further have

\bigtriangleup\eta^{k}=d\sum_{i=1}^{n}\frac{\partial}{\partial y^{i}}\mathcal{R}_{i}^{k}(B)+\vartheta_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}d\eta^{k}=\sum_{i,j=1}^{n}\frac{\partial^{2}}{\partial y^{j}\partial y^{i}}\mathcal{R}_{i}^{k}(B)dy^{j}+\sum_{j=1}^{n}\left\langle\vartheta_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}d\eta^{k},\frac{\partial}{\partial y^{j}}\right\rangle dy^{j}.

Here
\langle\cdot,\cdot\rangle
denotes the pairing between

1
forms and vector fields.

On the other hand,
\bigtriangleup\eta^{k}=\sum_{j=1}^{n}\bigtriangleup(\delta_{j}^{k}+b_{j}^{k})dy^{j}=\sum_{j=1}^{n}\bigtriangleup b_{j}^{k}dy^{j}
, therefore

\bigtriangleup b_{j}^{k}=\left\langle\bigtriangleup\eta^{k},\frac{\partial}{\partial y^{j}}\right\rangle=\sum_{i,j=1}^{n}\frac{\partial^{2}}{\partial y^{j}\partial y^{i}}\mathcal{R}_{i}^{k}(B)+\left\langle\vartheta_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}d\eta^{k},\frac{\partial}{\partial y^{j}}\right\rangle,\quad\text{in }\mathbb{B}^{n}_{y},\quad k=1,\dots,n.

(5.31)

Let
\tilde{\xi}_{0}:=\min(\tilde{C}_{\mathcal{R},\alpha}^{-1},\tilde{C}_{\mathcal{R},\beta}^{-1})
where
\tilde{C}_{\mathcal{R},\gamma}
is the constant in (
5.30
). Let
\xi=\xi_{B}\in(0,\tilde{\xi}_{0}]
to be determined. We define metric spaces
\mathscr{X}_{\gamma,\xi}
and an operator
\mathcal{T}_{B}:\mathscr{X}_{\alpha,\tilde{\xi}_{0}}\to\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}(\mathbb{B}^{n};\mathbb{M}^{n\times n})
by

\begin{gathered}\mathscr{X}_{\gamma,\xi}:=\{f\in\mathscr{C}^{\gamma}(\mathbb{B}^{n};\mathbb{M}^{n\times n}):\|f\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\gamma}}\leq\xi\}\subset\mathscr{C}^{\gamma}(\mathbb{B}^{n};\mathbb{M}^{n\times n}),\quad\text{for }\gamma\in\{\alpha,\beta\}\text{ and }\xi\in(0,\tilde{\xi}_{0}].\\
\mathcal{T}_{B}[f]^{k}_{j}:=b_{j}^{k}-\mathfrak{D}(\bigtriangleup b_{j}^{k})+\Big\langle\mathfrak{D}(\vartheta_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}d\eta^{k}),\frac{\partial}{\partial y^{j}}\Big\rangle+\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathfrak{D}\Big(\frac{\partial^{2}\mathcal{R}_{i}^{k}(f)}{\partial y^{j}\partial y^{i}}\Big),\quad 1\leq j,k\leq n.\end{gathered}

(5.32)

We endow
\mathscr{X}_{\gamma,\xi}
with the metric induced by the norm
\|\cdot\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\gamma}}
, which makes
\mathscr{X}_{\gamma,\xi}
a complete metric space.

Note that from (
5.32
) and (
5.31
) we have
B=\mathcal{T}_{B}[B]
. Our goal is to show that when
c_{3}
and
\xi
are both suitably small, we have
B\in\mathscr{X}_{\alpha,\xi}
and that
\mathcal{T}_{B}
is a contraction mapping on both
\mathscr{X}_{\alpha,\xi}
and
\mathscr{X}_{\beta,\xi}
, thus by uniqueness of the fixed point we conclude that
B
is a
\mathscr{C}^{\beta}
-matrix and
\|B\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\beta}}<\xi
.

By Lemma
5.7
,
\mathfrak{D}:\mathscr{C}^{\gamma-2}(\mathbb{B}^{n};\mathbb{M}^{n\times n})\to\mathscr{C}^{\gamma}(\mathbb{B}^{n};\mathbb{M}^{n\times n})
is bounded for
\gamma\in\{\alpha,\beta\}
. By (
5.30
), we know for every
f_{1},f_{2}\in\mathscr{X}_{\gamma,\xi}
,

\|\mathcal{T}_{B}[f_{1}]-\mathcal{T}_{B}[f_{2}]\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\gamma}}\leq\|\mathfrak{D}\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\gamma-2}\to\mathscr{C}^{\gamma}}\|\nabla^{2}\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\gamma}\to\mathscr{C}^{\gamma-2}}\sum_{k=1}^{n}\|\mathcal{R}_{k}^{l}(f_{1})-\mathcal{R}_{k}^{l}(f_{2})\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\gamma}}\leq\xi C^{\prime}_{\mathcal{R},\gamma}\|f_{1}-f_{2}\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\gamma}}.

(5.33)

where
C^{\prime}_{\mathcal{R},\gamma}>1
is a constant that only depends on
n,(\mathcal{R}_{j}^{k}),\gamma
but not on
B,\xi,f_{1},f_{2}
.

On the other hand
\mathcal{T}_{B}[0]_{j}^{k}=b_{j}^{k}-\mathfrak{D}(\bigtriangleup b_{j}^{k})+\left\langle\mathfrak{D}(\vartheta_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}d\eta^{k}),\frac{\partial}{\partial y^{j}}\right\rangle
. By (
5.25
), for
\gamma\in\{\alpha,\beta\}
,

\|\mathcal{T}_{B}[0]\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\gamma}}\leq\|\mathcal{T}_{B}[0]\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\beta}}\leq\|B-\mathfrak{D}(\bigtriangleup B)\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\beta}(\mathbb{B}^{n})}+\sum_{k=1}^{n}\|\mathfrak{D}(\vartheta_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}d\eta^{k})\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\beta}}\lesssim\|B\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\beta}(\partial\mathbb{B}^{n})}+\sum_{k=1}^{n}\|d\eta^{k}\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\beta-1}}.

(5.34)

So by possibly increasing
C^{\prime}_{\mathcal{R},\gamma}
, we have, for
f_{1}\in\mathscr{X}_{\gamma,\xi}
,
using (
5.33
) and (
5.34
),

\|\mathcal{T}_{B}[f_{1}]\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\gamma}}\leq\|\mathcal{T}_{B}[f_{1}]-\mathcal{T}_{B}[0]\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\gamma}}+\|\mathcal{T}_{B}[0]\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\gamma}}\leq C^{\prime}_{\mathcal{R},\gamma}\Big(\|B\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\beta}(\partial\mathbb{B}^{n})}+\sum_{l=1}^{n}\|d\eta^{l}\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\beta-1}}+\xi\|f_{1}\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\gamma}}\Big).

(5.35)

Take
c_{3}>0
satisfying
c_{3}<\frac{1}{4}\max(1,C^{\prime}_{\mathcal{R},\alpha},C^{\prime}_{\mathcal{R},\beta})^{-2}
, and take

\xi=\xi_{B}:=2\max(C^{\prime}_{\mathcal{R},\alpha},C^{\prime}_{\mathcal{R},\beta})\left(\|B\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}(\mathbb{B}^{n};\mathbb{M}^{n\times n})}+\|B\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\beta}(\partial\mathbb{B}^{n})}+\sum_{k=1}^{n}\|d\eta^{k}\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\beta-1}}\right).

(5.36)

By the assumption (
5.23
),
\xi_{B}\leq\frac{1}{2}\max(C^{\prime}_{\mathcal{R},\alpha},C^{\prime}_{\mathcal{R},\beta})^{-1}<\tilde{\xi}_{0}
, so
\mathcal{T}_{B}
is defined on
\mathscr{X}_{\alpha,\tilde{\xi}_{0}}
and by (
5.35
)
\mathcal{T}_{B}
maps
\mathscr{X}_{\gamma,\xi_{B}}
into
\mathscr{X}_{\gamma,\xi_{B}}
for
\gamma\in\{\alpha,\beta\}
.

Since
\xi_{B}C^{\prime}_{\mathcal{R},\gamma}<\frac{1}{2}
for
\gamma\in\{\alpha,\beta\}
, using (
5.33
),

\mathcal{T}_{B}
is a contraction mapping on the domain
\mathscr{X}_{\gamma,\xi_{B}}
, for
\gamma\in\{\alpha,\beta\}
.

Note that
\xi_{B}\geq\|B\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}}
, and so
B\in\{f\in\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}(\mathbb{B}^{n};\mathbb{M}^{n\times n}):\|f\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}}\leq\xi_{B}\}=\mathscr{X}_{\alpha,\xi_{B}}
. Therefore,
B
is a fixed point for
\mathcal{T}_{B}
in
\mathscr{X}_{\alpha,\xi_{B}}
, which is unique since
\mathcal{T}_{B}
is a contraction mapping on
\mathscr{X}_{\alpha,\xi_{B}}
.

On the other hand
\mathcal{T}_{B}
also has a unique fixed point in
\mathscr{X}_{\beta,\xi_{B}}\subsetneq\mathscr{X}_{\alpha,\xi_{B}}
. Therefore, by uniqueness,
B\in\mathscr{X}_{\beta,\xi_{B}}=\{f\in\mathscr{C}^{\beta}(\mathbb{B}^{n};\mathbb{M}^{n\times n}):\|f\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\beta}}\leq\xi_{B}\}
.
In particular,
\|B\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\beta}}\leq\xi_{B}
. Thus by (
5.36
),

\|B\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\beta}(\mathbb{B}^{n})}\leq\xi_{B}\lesssim_{\alpha,\beta}\|B\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}(\mathbb{B}^{n})}+\|B\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\beta}(\partial\mathbb{B}^{n})}+\sum_{l=1}^{n}\|d\eta^{l}\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\beta-1}}.

Thus, we have established (
5.24
)
which completes the proof.
∎

5.4
The proof of Theorem
5.1
and an improvement

Using Propositions
5.10
and
5.14
we can prove Theorem
5.1
.

Proof of Theorem
5.1
.

Let
c_{1},c_{2},c_{3}>0
be the small constants in Proposition
5.10
, Lemma
5.13
, and Proposition
5.14
. We take
c=\frac{1}{2n^{2}}\min(c_{1},c_{2}c_{3})
in the assumption of Theorem
5.1
.

Let
F
,
R=F-\mathrm{id}
,
\Phi=F^{-1}
,
A
,
B
and
\eta^{i}=F_{*}\lambda^{i}
be as in Proposition
5.10
. Recall
\eta^{i}
and
B=(b_{i}^{j})
are given in (
5.3
) and (
5.4
).

When the assumption (
5.1
) is satisfied, by Proposition
5.10

(ii)
we have that
F
is
\mathscr{C}^{\alpha+1}
-diffeomorphism and satisfies
B^{n}(F(0),\frac{1}{6})\subseteq F(\frac{1}{3}\mathbb{B}^{n})\cap\frac{3}{4}\mathbb{B}^{n}
. And by (
5.12
), we have
\|F-\mathrm{id}\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\alpha+1}}=\|R\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\alpha+1}}\leq c_{1}^{-1}\|A\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}}\leq\frac{1}{2n}c^{-1}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\|\lambda^{i}-dx^{i}\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}}
. This implies half of the estimate (
5.2
).

By Lemma
5.13

2
and
3
, we have
\|B\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}(\mathbb{B}^{n})}+\|B\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\beta}(\partial\mathbb{B}^{n})}<c_{2}^{-1}\|A\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}}
and
\|d\eta^{k}\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\beta-1}}<c_{2}^{-1}\|d\lambda^{k}\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\beta-1}}
,
k=1,\dots,n
.

Thus,
\|B\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}(\mathbb{B}^{n})}+\|B\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\beta}(\partial\mathbb{B}^{n})}+\sum_{k=1}^{n}\|d\eta^{k}\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\beta-1}}<c_{2}^{-1}\|A\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}}+c_{2}^{-1}\sum_{k=1}^{n}\|d\lambda^{k}\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\beta-1}}<2c_{2}^{-1}c<c_{3}
.
By (
5.24
) in Proposition
5.14
, we get

\displaystyle\sum_{k=1}^{n}\|\eta^{k}-dy^{k}\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\beta}}\leq n\|B\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\beta}(\mathbb{B}^{n})}\leq nc_{3}^{-1}\left(\|B\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}}+\|B\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\beta}(\partial\mathbb{B}^{n})}+\sum_{k=1}^{n}\|d\eta^{k}\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\beta-1}}\right)

\displaystyle\leq nc_{3}^{-1}\cdot c_{2}^{-1}\left(\|A\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}}+\sum_{k=1}^{n}\|d\lambda^{k}\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\beta-1}}\right)\leq n^{2}(c_{2}c_{3})^{-1}\sum_{k=1}^{n}\left(\|\lambda^{k}-dx^{k}\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}}+\|d\lambda^{k}\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\beta-1}}\right).

This gives the second half of the estimate (
5.2
) since
n^{2}(c_{2}c_{3})^{-1}\leq\frac{1}{2}c^{-1}
.
∎

In Theorem
5.1
, we assumed (
5.1
) which is a smallness assumption. When (
5.1
)
is not satisfied, we may use a scaling argument to transfer to a setting where it is satisfied, as the next result shows.

Proposition 5.15

(The scaling argument)
.

Let
\alpha>0
,
\beta\in[\alpha,\alpha+1]

and let
\mu_{0},\tilde{c},M>0
. There exists a
\kappa_{0}=\kappa_{0}(\alpha,\beta,\mu_{0},\tilde{c},M)\in(0,\mu_{0}]
that satisfies the following:

Suppose
\theta^{1},\dots,\theta^{n}\in\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}(\mu_{0}\mathbb{B}^{n};T^{*}\mathbb{R}^{n})
such that
\theta^{i}\big|_{0}=dx^{i}\big|_{0}
for
i=1,\dots,n
and
d\theta^{1},\dots,d\theta^{n}\in\mathscr{C}^{\beta-1}\left(\mu_{0}\mathbb{B}^{n};{\textstyle\bigwedge}^{2}T^{*}\mathbb{R}^{n}\right)
with estimate

\sum_{i=1}^{n}\|\theta^{i}\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}(\mu_{0}\mathbb{B}^{n};T^{*}\mathbb{R}^{n})}+\|d\theta^{i}\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\beta-1}(\mu_{0}\mathbb{B}^{n};\wedge^{2}T^{*}\mathbb{R}^{n})}<M.

(5.37)

Then there are 1-forms
\lambda^{1},\dots,\lambda^{n}\in\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}(\mathbb{B}^{n};T^{*}\mathbb{B}^{n})
such that

(i)

\lambda^{i}\big|_{\frac{1}{3}\mathbb{B}^{n}}=\frac{1}{\kappa_{0}}\cdot(\phi_{\kappa_{0}}^{*}\theta^{i})\big|_{\frac{1}{3}\mathbb{B}^{n}}
,
i=1,\dots,n
, where
\phi_{\kappa_{0}}(x):=\kappa_{0}\cdot x
is the scaling map.

(ii)

\lambda^{1},\dots,\lambda^{n}
satisfy the assumptions of Theorem
5.1
with the constant
c=\tilde{c}
. That is,

•

\lambda^{1},\dots,\lambda^{n}
span the cotangent space at every point in
\mathbb{B}^{n}
.

•

\operatorname{supp}(\lambda^{i}-dx^{i})\subsetneq\frac{1}{2}\mathbb{B}^{n}
.

•

\sum_{i=1}^{n}(\|\lambda^{i}-dx^{i}\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}}+\|d\lambda^{i}\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\beta-1}})\leq\tilde{c}
.

The key to Proposition
5.15
is the next lemma.

Lemma 5.16
.

Let
\gamma>0
, then for any
\mu_{0}>0
there is a
C_{\gamma,\mu_{0}}>0
such that,

\|f(\kappa\cdot)\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\gamma}(\mathbb{B}^{n})}\leq C_{\gamma,\mu_{0}}\kappa^{\min(\gamma,\frac{1}{2})}\|f\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\gamma}(\mu_{0}\mathbb{B}^{n})},\quad\forall\kappa\in(0,\mu_{0}],f\in\mathscr{C}^{\gamma}(\mu_{0}\mathbb{B}^{n})\text{ such that }f(0)=0.

(5.38)

Proof.

By taking a scaling
x\mapsto\mu_{0}x
, we can assume
\mu_{0}=1
without loss of generality. Thus,
f
is defined on the unit ball.
To prove the result, we use the characterizations of Zygmund-Hölder norms in Remark
2.1
.

For
\kappa\in(0,1]
set
f_{\kappa}(x):=f(\kappa x)
.
For
x\in\mathbb{B}^{n}
and
\kappa\in(0,1]
, by Remark
2.1

(ii)
, for
x\in\mathbb{B}^{n}
,

|f_{\kappa}(x)|=|f(\kappa x)-f(0)|\lesssim\|f\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\min(\gamma,\frac{1}{2})}(\mathbb{B}^{n})}|\kappa x-0|^{\min(\gamma,\frac{1}{2})}\lesssim\|f\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\gamma}(\mathbb{B}^{n})}\kappa^{\min(\gamma,\frac{1}{2})}.

(5.39)

When
\gamma\in(0,2)
, using Remark
2.1

(i)
, for
x_{1},x_{2}\in\mathbb{B}^{n}
,

\textstyle|\frac{f_{\kappa}(x_{1})+f_{\kappa}(x_{2})}{2}-f_{\kappa}(\frac{x_{1}+x_{2}}{2})|=\left|\frac{f(\kappa x_{1})+f(\kappa x_{2})}{2}-f(\kappa\frac{x_{1}+x_{2}}{2})\right|\lesssim_{\gamma}\|f\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\min(\gamma,\frac{1}{2})}}|\kappa(x_{1}-x_{2})|^{\gamma}\leq\kappa^{\gamma}\|f\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\gamma}}|x_{1}-x_{2}|^{\gamma}.

(5.40)

Combining (
5.39
) and (
5.40
), we get (
5.38
) for the case
0<\gamma<2
, since

\textstyle\|f_{\kappa}\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\gamma}(\mathbb{B}^{n})}\approx\sup\limits_{x\in\mathbb{B}^{n}}|f_{\kappa}(x)|+\sup\limits_{x_{1},x_{2}\in\mathbb{B}^{n}}|x_{1}-x_{2}|^{-\gamma}\left|\frac{f_{\kappa}(x_{1})+f_{\kappa}(x_{2})}{2}-f_{\kappa}(\frac{x_{1}+x_{2}}{2})\right|\lesssim_{\gamma}\kappa^{\min(\gamma,\frac{1}{2})}\|f\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\gamma}(\mathbb{B}^{n})}.

For
\gamma\geq 2
, we proceed by induction. We prove the result for
\gamma\in[l,l+1)
, for
l\in\{1,2,\ldots\}
. The base case,
l=1
was shown above. We assume the result for
l-1
and prove it for
l
.

Assume
\gamma\in[l,l+1)
where
l\geq 2
. Note that
\nabla f_{\kappa}(x)=\kappa(\nabla f)(\kappa x)
, so
\|\partial_{x^{j}}(f_{\kappa})\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\gamma-1}(\mathbb{B}^{n})}=\kappa\|(\partial_{x^{j}}f)_{\kappa}\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\gamma-1}(\mathbb{B}^{n})}\leq\|(\partial_{x^{j}}f)_{\kappa}\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\gamma-1}(\mathbb{B}^{n})}
for
j=1,\dots,n
. Here
(\partial_{x^{j}}f)_{\kappa}(x)=(\partial_{x^{j}}f)(\kappa x)
.

By the inductive hypothesis
\|f_{\kappa}\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\gamma-1}(\mathbb{B}^{n})}\leq C_{\gamma-1}\kappa^{\frac{1}{2}}\|f\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\gamma-1}(\mathbb{B}^{n})}
and
\|(\partial_{x^{j}}f)_{\kappa}\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\gamma-1}(\mathbb{B}^{n})}\leq C_{\gamma-1}\kappa^{\frac{1}{2}}\|\partial_{x^{j}}f\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\gamma-1}(\mathbb{B}^{n})}
for
j=1,\dots,n
. So by Remark
2.1

(iii)
we get

\displaystyle\|f_{\kappa}\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\gamma}(\mathbb{B}^{n})}\approx\|f_{\kappa}\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\gamma-1}(\mathbb{B}^{n})}+\sum_{j=1}^{n}\|\partial_{x^{j}}(f_{\kappa})\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\gamma-1}(\mathbb{B}^{n})}

\displaystyle\lesssim\kappa^{\frac{1}{2}}\Big(\|f\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\gamma-1}(\mathbb{B}^{n})}+\sum_{j=1}^{n}\|\partial_{x^{j}}f\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\gamma-1}(\mathbb{B}^{n})}\Big)\approx\kappa^{\frac{1}{2}}\|f\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\gamma}(\mathbb{B}^{n})}=\kappa^{\min(\gamma,\frac{1}{2})}\|f\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\gamma}(\mathbb{B}^{n})},

completing the proof.
∎

Proof of Proposition
5.15
.

First we construct 1-forms
\rho^{1},\dots,\rho^{n}\in\mathscr{C}^{\beta}(\mu_{0}\mathbb{B}^{n};T^{*}\mathbb{R}^{n})
such that for
i=1,\dots,n
,

(a)

\rho^{i}\big|_{0}=0
and
d\rho^{i}\big|_{\frac{\mu_{0}}{2}\mathbb{B}^{n}}=d\theta^{i}\big|_{\frac{\mu_{0}}{2}\mathbb{B}^{n}}
.

(b)

There is a
C_{0}=C_{0}(n,\alpha,\beta,\mu_{0})>0
that does not depend on
\theta^{i}
, such that

\|\rho^{i}\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\beta}(\mu_{0}\mathbb{B}^{n};T^{*}\mathbb{R}^{n})}\leq C_{0}(\|\theta^{i}\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}(\mu_{0}\mathbb{B}^{n};T^{*}\mathbb{R}^{n})}+\|d\theta^{i}\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\beta-1}(\mu_{0}\mathbb{B}^{n};\wedge^{2}T^{*}\mathbb{R}^{n})}).

(5.41)

Take a
\chi_{0}\in C_{c}^{\infty}(\mu_{0}\mathbb{B}^{n})
such that
\chi_{0}\big|_{\frac{\mu_{0}}{2}\mathbb{B}^{n}}\equiv 1
. Define

\tilde{\rho}^{i}:=\mathcal{G}\ast\vartheta d(\chi_{0}\theta^{i})=\mathcal{G}\ast\vartheta(\chi_{0}\cdot d\theta^{i}+d\chi_{0}\wedge\theta^{i}),\quad\rho^{i}:=\tilde{\rho}^{i}-(\tilde{\rho}^{i}\big|_{0}),\quad i=1,\dots,n.

(5.42)

Recall
\vartheta
is the codifferential from Notation
4.5
, and
\mathcal{G}
is the fundamental solution of Laplacian as in (
4.6
).
The convolution is defined in
\mathbb{R}^{n}
using Lemma
4.6
, since the support
\operatorname{supp}\vartheta d(\chi_{0}\theta^{i})\subseteq\operatorname{supp}\chi_{0}\Subset\mu_{0}\mathbb{B}^{n}
is compact.

Clearly
\rho^{i}\big|_{0}=0
. Similar to the proof of Lemma
4.8
, since
\chi_{0}\big|_{\frac{\mu_{0}}{2}\mathbb{B}^{n}}\equiv 1
, we have

d\theta^{i}\big|_{\frac{\mu_{0}}{2}\mathbb{B}^{n}}=d(\chi_{0}\theta^{i})\big|_{\frac{\mu_{0}}{2}\mathbb{B}^{n}}=(\vartheta d+d\vartheta)(\mathcal{G}\ast d(\chi_{0}\theta^{i}))\big|_{\frac{\mu_{0}}{2}\mathbb{B}^{n}}=(\mathcal{G}\ast d\vartheta d(\chi_{0}\theta^{i}))\big|_{\frac{\mu_{0}}{2}\mathbb{B}^{n}}=d\tilde{\rho}^{i}\big|_{\frac{\mu_{0}}{2}\mathbb{B}^{n}}=d\rho^{i}\big|_{\frac{\mu_{0}}{2}\mathbb{B}^{n}}.

So condition
(a)
is satisfied.

By Lemma
4.7
we have, for every
\mu>0
,

\|\mathcal{G}\ast\vartheta\omega\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\beta}(\mu\mathbb{B}^{n};\wedge^{2}T^{*}\mathbb{R}^{n})}\lesssim_{\beta,\mu}\|\omega\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\beta-1}(\mu\mathbb{B}^{n};T^{*}\mathbb{R}^{n})},\quad\forall\omega\in\mathscr{C}^{\beta-1}_{c}(\mu\mathbb{B}^{n};T^{*}\mathbb{R}^{n}).

(5.43)

Take
\omega=\vartheta(\chi_{0}\cdot d\theta^{i}+d\chi_{0}\wedge\theta^{i})
,
\mu=\mu_{0}
in (
5.43
) and by Lemma
2.2
, we have

\displaystyle\|\rho^{i}\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\beta}(\mu_{0}\mathbb{B}^{n};T^{*}\mathbb{R}^{n})}\leq\|\tilde{\rho}^{i}\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\beta}(\mu_{0}\mathbb{B}^{n};T^{*}\mathbb{R}^{n})}+|\tilde{\rho}^{i}(0)|\leq 2\|\tilde{\rho}^{i}\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\beta}(\mu_{0}\mathbb{B}^{n};T^{*}\mathbb{R}^{n})}

(5.44)

\displaystyle\lesssim_{\beta,\mu_{0}}\|\vartheta(\chi_{0}\cdot d\theta^{i}+d\chi_{0}\wedge\theta^{i})\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\beta-2}(\mu_{0}\mathbb{B}^{n};\wedge^{2}T^{*}\mathbb{R}^{n})}\lesssim_{\beta,\mu_{0}}\|\chi_{0}\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\alpha+1}}\|d\theta^{i}\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\beta-1}}+\|d\chi_{0}\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}}\|\theta^{i}\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\beta-1}}

\displaystyle\lesssim_{\alpha,\beta,\mu_{0},\chi_{0}}\|d\theta^{i}\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\beta-1}(\mu_{0}\mathbb{B}^{n};\wedge^{2}T^{*}\mathbb{R}^{n})}+\|\theta^{i}\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}(\mu_{0}\mathbb{B}^{n};T^{*}\mathbb{R}^{n})}.

(
5.44
) gives us the
C_{0}
for condition
(b)
. This complete the proof of
(a)
and
(b)
and we get
\rho^{1},\dots,\rho^{n}
as desired.

Fix
\chi_{1}\in C_{c}^{\infty}(\frac{1}{2}\mathbb{B}^{n})
such that
\chi_{1}\big|_{\frac{1}{3}\mathbb{B}^{n}}\equiv 1
. For
\kappa>0
, let
\phi_{\kappa}(x):=\kappa\cdot x
, so
\phi_{\kappa}
maps
\frac{1}{2}\mathbb{B}^{n}
into
\frac{\mu_{0}}{2}\mathbb{B}^{n}
when
\kappa\in(0,\mu_{0}]
. For
\kappa\in(0,\mu_{0}]
, we define 1-forms
\lambda_{\kappa}^{1},\dots,\lambda_{\kappa}^{n}
and
\tau_{\kappa}^{1},\dots,\tau_{\kappa}^{n}
by

\lambda_{\kappa}^{i}:=dx^{i}+\tfrac{1}{\kappa}\chi_{1}\cdot\phi_{\kappa}^{*}(\theta^{i}-dx^{i}),\quad\tau_{\kappa}^{i}:=\tfrac{1}{\kappa}\chi_{1}\cdot(\phi_{\kappa}^{*}\rho^{i})+\tfrac{1}{\kappa}\mathcal{G}\ast\vartheta\left(d\chi_{1}\wedge\phi_{\kappa}^{*}(\theta^{i}-dx^{i})\right),\quad i=1,\dots,n.

(5.45)

Since
\theta^{i}\in\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}
and
\rho^{i}\in\mathscr{C}^{\beta}
, we have
\lambda_{\kappa}^{i}\in\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}(\mathbb{B}^{n};T^{*}\mathbb{R}^{n})
,
\tau_{\kappa}^{i}\in\mathscr{C}^{\beta}(\mathbb{B}^{n};T^{*}\mathbb{R}^{n})
(by (
5.43
)) and
\operatorname{supp}(\lambda_{\kappa}^{i}-dx^{i})\subseteq\operatorname{supp}\chi_{1}\Subset\frac{1}{2}\mathbb{B}^{n}
. And since
\chi_{1}\big|_{\frac{1}{3}\mathbb{B}^{n}}\equiv 1
and
\phi_{\kappa}^{*}dx=\kappa dx
,

\lambda_{\kappa}^{i}\big|_{\frac{1}{3}\mathbb{B}^{n}}=dx^{i}+\tfrac{1}{\kappa}\cdot\phi_{\kappa}^{*}(\theta^{i}-dx^{i})\big|_{\frac{1}{3}\mathbb{B}^{n}}=\tfrac{1}{\kappa}(\phi_{\kappa}^{*}\theta^{i})\big|_{\frac{1}{3}\mathbb{B}^{n}}.

(5.46)

We write
\theta^{i}
and
\rho^{i}
,
i=1,\dots,n
as

\theta^{i}=dx^{i}+\sum_{j=1}^{n}a^{i}_{j}(x)dx^{j},\quad\rho^{i}=\sum_{j=1}^{n}b^{i}_{j}(x)dx^{j},\quad\text{where }a^{i}_{j}\in\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}(\mu_{0}\mathbb{B}^{n}),\ b^{i}_{j}\in\mathscr{C}^{\beta}(\mu_{0}\mathbb{B}^{n}).

(5.47)

By assumption
\theta^{i}\big|_{0}=dx^{i}\big|_{0}
and
\rho^{i}\big|_{0}=0
for
i=1,\dots,n
, so
a^{i}_{j}(0)=b^{i}_{j}(0)=0
for all
1\leq i,j\leq n
. And we have

\lambda_{\kappa}^{i}=dx^{i}+\sum_{j=1}^{n}\chi_{1}(x)a^{i}_{j}(\kappa x)dx^{j},\quad\tau_{\kappa}^{i}=\sum_{j=1}^{n}\Big(\chi_{1}(x)b^{i}_{j}(\kappa x)dx^{j}+\mathcal{G}\ast\vartheta\big(a^{i}_{j}(\kappa x)d\chi_{1}\wedge dx^{j}\big)\Big),\quad i=1,\dots,n.

(5.48)

Since
\phi_{\kappa}(\frac{1}{2}\mathbb{B}^{n})\subseteq\frac{\mu_{0}}{2}\mathbb{B}^{n}
and
\operatorname{supp}\chi_{1}\Subset\frac{1}{2}\mathbb{B}^{n}
, by condition
(a)
we have
\chi_{1}\cdot\phi_{\kappa}^{*}d\rho^{i}=\chi_{1}\cdot\phi_{\kappa}^{*}d\theta^{i}
for
i=1,\dots,n
. It follows that
d\lambda_{\kappa}^{i}=d\tau_{\kappa}^{i}
for
i=1,\dots,n
; indeed,

d\lambda_{\kappa}^{i}-d\tau_{\kappa}^{i}=\tfrac{1}{\kappa}d\chi_{1}\wedge\phi_{\kappa}^{*}(\theta^{i}-dx^{i})+\tfrac{1}{\kappa}\chi_{1}\cdot\phi_{\kappa}^{*}d\theta^{i}-\tfrac{1}{\kappa}d\chi_{1}\wedge\phi_{\kappa}^{*}(\theta^{i}-dx^{i})-\tfrac{1}{\kappa}\chi_{1}\cdot\phi_{\kappa}^{*}d\rho^{i}=\tfrac{1}{\kappa}\chi_{1}\cdot\phi_{\kappa}^{*}(d\theta^{i}-d\rho^{i})=0.

(5.49)

Applying Lemmas
2.2
and
5.16
to
a^{i}_{j}
we have

\|\chi_{1}\cdot a^{i}_{j}(\kappa\cdot)\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}(\mathbb{B}^{n})}\lesssim_{\alpha}\|\chi_{1}\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}}\|a^{i}_{j}(\kappa\cdot)\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}(\mathbb{B}^{n})}\lesssim_{\alpha,\mu_{0}}\kappa^{\min(\alpha,\frac{1}{2})}\|\chi_{1}\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}}\|a^{i}_{j}\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}(\mu_{0}\mathbb{B}^{n})},\quad\forall\kappa\in(0,\mu_{0}].

(5.50)

Using (
5.48
) and (
5.50
), we deduce that

\sum_{i=1}^{n}\|\lambda_{\kappa}^{i}-dx^{i}\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}}\leq\sum_{i,j=1}^{n}\|\chi_{1}(x)a^{i}_{j}(\kappa x)\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}}\lesssim_{\alpha,\mu_{0}}\kappa^{\min(\alpha,\frac{1}{2})}\|\chi_{1}\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}}\sum_{i,j=1}^{n}\|a^{i}_{j}\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}(\mu_{0}\mathbb{B}^{n})}.

(5.51)

By (
5.49
) we have
d\lambda_{\kappa}^{i}=d\tau_{\kappa}^{i}
, and therefore

\|d\lambda_{\kappa}^{i}\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\beta-1}(\mathbb{B}^{n};\wedge^{2}T^{*}\mathbb{B}^{n})}=\|d\tau_{\kappa}^{i}\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\beta-1}(\mathbb{B}^{n};\wedge^{2}T^{*}\mathbb{B}^{n})}\lesssim_{\beta}\|\tau_{\kappa}^{i}\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\beta}(\mathbb{B}^{n};T^{*}\mathbb{B}^{n})},\quad i=1,\dots,n.

(5.52)

Applying Lemma
5.16
to
a^{i}_{j}
and
b^{i}_{j}
we get that for
0<\kappa<\mu_{0}
,

\displaystyle\|\chi_{1}\cdot b^{i}_{j}(\kappa x)\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\beta}(\mathbb{B}^{n})}\lesssim_{\beta}\|\chi_{1}\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\beta}}\|b^{i}_{j}(\kappa x)\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\beta}(\mathbb{B}^{n})}\lesssim_{\beta}\kappa^{\min(\beta,\frac{1}{2})}\|\chi_{1}\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\beta}}\|b^{i}_{j}\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\beta}}\leq\kappa^{\min(\alpha,\frac{1}{2})}\|\chi_{1}\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\beta}}\|b^{i}_{j}\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\beta}(\mu_{0}\mathbb{B}^{n})}.

(5.53)

\displaystyle\|a^{i}_{j}(\kappa x)d\chi_{1}\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}(\mathbb{B}^{n};T^{*}\mathbb{R}^{n})}\lesssim_{\alpha}\|a^{i}_{j}(\kappa x)\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}(\mathbb{B}^{n})}\|\chi_{1}\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\alpha+1}}\lesssim_{\alpha}\kappa^{\min(\alpha,\frac{1}{2})}\|\chi_{1}\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\alpha+1}}\|a^{i}_{j}\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}(\mu_{0}\mathbb{B}^{n})}.

(5.54)

Letting
\omega=a_{j}^{i}(\kappa x)d\chi_{1}\wedge dx^{j}\in\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}(\mathbb{B}^{n};T^{*}\mathbb{B}^{n})\subseteq\mathscr{C}^{\beta-1}(\mathbb{B}^{n};T^{*}\mathbb{B}^{n})
and
\mu=1
in (
5.43
), we see that

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{n}\|d\lambda_{\kappa}^{i}\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\beta-1}(\mathbb{B}^{n};\wedge^{2}T^{*}\mathbb{B}^{n})}=\sum_{i=1}^{n}\|d\tau_{\kappa}^{i}\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\beta-1}(\mathbb{B}^{n};\wedge^{2}T^{*}\mathbb{B}^{n})}\lesssim_{\beta}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\|\tau_{\kappa}^{i}\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\beta}(\mathbb{B}^{n};T^{*}\mathbb{B}^{n})}

\displaystyle\text{by \eqref{Eqn::Key::ScalingTmpDlambdaDkappa}}

(5.55)

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{i,j=1}^{n}\big(\|\chi_{1}(x)b_{j}^{i}(\kappa x)dx^{j}\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\beta}}+\big\|\mathcal{G}\ast\vartheta\big(a^{i}_{j}(\kappa x)d\chi_{1}\wedge dx^{j}\big)\big\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\beta}}\big)

\displaystyle\text{by \eqref{Eqn::Key::ScalingProofLambdaCoordinate}}

\displaystyle\lesssim_{\beta}\sum_{i,j=1}^{n}\big(\|\chi_{1}(x)b_{j}^{i}(\kappa x)\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\beta}(\mathbb{B}^{n})}+\|a^{i}_{j}(\kappa x)d\chi_{1}\wedge dx^{j}\big\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\beta-1}(\mathbb{B}^{n};\wedge^{2}T^{*}\mathbb{B}^{n})}\big)

\displaystyle\text{by \eqref{Eqn::Key::ScalingProof3}}

\displaystyle\lesssim_{\alpha,\beta}\|\chi_{1}\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\alpha+1}}\sum_{i,j=1}^{n}\big(\|b_{j}^{i}(\kappa x)\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\beta}(\mathbb{B}^{n})}+\|a^{i}_{j}(\kappa x)d\chi_{1}\big\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}(\mathbb{B}^{n};T^{*}\mathbb{B}^{n})}\big)

\displaystyle\text{since }\alpha\geq\beta-1

\displaystyle\lesssim_{\alpha,\beta}\kappa^{\min(\alpha,\frac{1}{2})}\|\chi_{1}\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\alpha+1}}\sum_{i,j=1}^{n}\Big(\|a^{i}_{j}\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}(\mu_{0}\mathbb{B}^{n})}+\|b^{i}_{j}\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\beta}(\mu_{0}\mathbb{B}^{n})}\Big)

\displaystyle\text{by \eqref{Eqn::Key::ScalingTmp2.1} and \eqref{Eqn::Key::ScalingTmp2.2}}.

Note that
\chi_{1}
is a fixed cut-off function whose
\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}
and
\mathscr{C}^{\alpha+1}
-norms depend only on
n,\alpha
. So combining (
5.51
) and (
5.55
) we have

\sum_{i=1}^{n}(\|\lambda_{\kappa}^{i}-dx^{i}\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}}+\|d\lambda_{\kappa}^{i}\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\beta-1}})\lesssim_{\alpha,\beta,\mu_{0}}\kappa^{\min(\alpha,\frac{1}{2})}\sum_{i,j=1}^{n}\big(\|a^{i}_{j}\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}(\mu_{0}\mathbb{B}^{n};T^{*}\mathbb{R}^{n})}+\|b^{i}_{j}\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\beta}(\mu_{0}\mathbb{B}^{n};\wedge^{2}T^{*}\mathbb{R}^{n})}\big).

(5.56)

By (
5.47
) we have
\sum_{i,j=1}^{n}\|a^{i}_{j}\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}}\lesssim 1+\sum_{i=1}^{n}\|\theta^{i}\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}}
and
\sum_{i,j=1}^{n}\|b^{i}_{j}\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\beta}}\lesssim\sum_{i=1}^{n}\|\rho^{i}\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\beta}}
. And combining (
5.56
) with (
5.41
), we can find a
C_{1}=C_{1}(n,\alpha,\beta,\mu_{0})>0
that does not depend on the other quantities, such that

\sum_{i=1}^{n}\|\lambda_{\kappa}^{i}-dx^{i}\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}}+\|d\lambda_{\kappa}^{i}\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\beta-1}}\leq C_{1}\cdot\kappa^{\min(\alpha,\frac{1}{2})}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\Big(1+\|\theta^{i}\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}(\mu_{0}\mathbb{B}^{n};T^{*}\mathbb{R}^{n})}+\|d\theta^{i}\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\beta-1}(\mu_{0}\mathbb{B}^{n};\wedge^{2}T^{*}\mathbb{R}^{n})}\Big),\quad\forall\kappa\in(0,\mu_{0}].

(5.57)

Now applying assumption (
5.37
) to (
5.57
) we have

\sum_{i=1}^{n}\|\lambda_{\kappa}^{i}-dx^{i}\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}}+\|d\lambda_{\kappa}^{i}\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\beta-1}}\leq\kappa^{\min(\alpha,\frac{1}{2})}C_{1}\cdot(M+n).

Since
\|(\langle\lambda_{\kappa}^{i}-dx^{i},\frac{\partial}{\partial x^{j}}\rangle)_{n\times n}\|_{C^{0}(\mathbb{B}^{n};\mathbb{M}^{n\times n})}\lesssim_{\alpha}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\|\lambda_{\kappa}^{i}-dx^{i}\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}(\mathbb{B}^{n})}
, we can find a
\tilde{c}^{\prime}=\tilde{c}^{\prime}(n,\alpha)>0
such that

\sum_{i=1}^{n}\|\lambda_{\kappa}^{i}-dx^{i}\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}}\leq\tilde{c}^{\prime}\quad\text{implies}\quad\textstyle\|(\langle\lambda_{\kappa}^{i}-dx^{i},\frac{\partial}{\partial x^{j}}\rangle)_{n\times n}\|_{C^{0}(\mathbb{B}^{n};\mathbb{M}^{n\times n})}\leq\frac{1}{2}.

In particular
\left(\langle\lambda_{\kappa}^{i},\frac{\partial}{\partial x^{j}}\rangle\right)_{n\times n}=I+\left(\langle\lambda_{\kappa}^{i}-dx^{i},\frac{\partial}{\partial x^{j}}\rangle\right)_{n\times n}
is invertible at every point in
\mathbb{B}^{n}
, which means
(\lambda_{\kappa}^{1},\dots,\lambda_{\kappa}^{n})
span the tangent space at every point in
\mathbb{B}^{n}
.

We take
\kappa_{0}=\kappa_{0}(n,\alpha,\beta,\mu_{0},M,\tilde{c})>0
such that

0<\kappa_{0}<\mu_{0}\quad\text{and}\quad\kappa_{0}^{\min(\alpha,\frac{1}{2})}C_{1}\cdot(M+n)\leq\min(\tilde{c},\tilde{c}^{\prime}).

Take
\lambda^{i}=\lambda_{\kappa_{0}}^{i}
for
i=1,\dots,n
. We have
\sum_{i=1}^{n}\|\lambda^{i}-dx^{i}\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}}+\|d\lambda^{i}\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\beta-1}}\leq\min(\tilde{c},\tilde{c}^{\prime})
. By our assumption on
\tilde{c}^{\prime}
,
\lambda^{1},\dots,\lambda^{n}
span the tangent space at every point in
\mathbb{B}^{n}
. Note that by (
5.48
)
\operatorname{supp}(\lambda_{\kappa_{0}}^{i}-dx^{i})\subseteq\operatorname{supp}\chi_{1}\Subset\frac{1}{2}\mathbb{B}^{n}
. This shows conclusion
(ii)
is satisfied.

By (
5.46
) we get conclusion
(i)
, finishing the proof.
∎

We can now prove a special case of the Theorem
3.1
:

Corollary 5.17
.

Let
\alpha>0
and let
\beta\in[\alpha,\alpha+1]
. Let
\lambda^{1},\dots,\lambda^{n}
be
\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}
1-forms on a
\mathscr{C}^{\alpha+1}
-manifold
\mathfrak{M}
of dimension
n
, which span the cotangent space at every point. Then the following are equivalent:

1.

For every
p\in\mathfrak{M}
there exist a neighborhood
U\subseteq\mathfrak{M}
of
p
and a
\mathscr{C}^{\alpha+1}_{\mathrm{loc}}
-diffeomorphism
\Phi:\mathbb{B}^{n}\xrightarrow{\sim}U\subseteq\mathfrak{M}

such that
\Phi(0)=p
and
\Phi^{*}\lambda^{1},\dots,\Phi^{*}\lambda^{n}\in\mathscr{C}^{\beta}(\mathbb{B}^{n};T^{*}\mathbb{B}^{n})
.

2.

d\lambda^{1},\dots,d\lambda^{n}
have regularity
\mathscr{C}^{\beta-1}_{\mathrm{loc}}
(see Definition
2.9
).

Remark 5.18
.

This is the special case of Theorem
3.1
when
\beta\in[\alpha,\alpha+1]
and
q=n
: given
\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}
-vector fields
X_{1},\dots,X_{n}
that span the
n
-dimensional tangent space at every point, we take 1-forms
\lambda^{1},\dots,\lambda^{n}
to be the corresponding dual basis that span the cotangent space at every point.

Proof.

By passing to a local coordinate system, we can assume
\mathfrak{M}
to be an open subset of
\mathbb{R}^{n}
, since by Proposition
4.10
both conditions
1
and
2
are invariant under
\mathscr{C}^{\alpha+1}_{\mathrm{loc}}
-diffeomorphisms.

1
\Rightarrow
2
: For such
\Phi
, we have
d(\Phi^{*}\lambda^{i})\in\mathscr{C}^{\beta-1}_{\mathrm{loc}}(\mathbb{B}^{n};T^{*}\mathbb{B}^{n})
for
i=1,\dots,n
. So
\Phi^{-1}:U\subseteq\mathfrak{M}\to\mathbb{R}^{n}
is the desired coordinate chart that shows
d\lambda^{1},\dots,d\lambda^{n}
fulfill the
conditions for
d\lambda^{1},\dots,d\lambda^{n}
to have regularity in
\mathscr{C}^{\beta-1}_{\mathrm{loc}}

(see Definition
2.9
).

2
\Rightarrow
1
: Let
p\in\mathfrak{M}
.

By passing to local coordinate system and applying an invertible linear transformation we can find a
\mu_{0}>0
and a
\mathscr{C}^{\alpha+1}
-coordinate chart
F_{0}:U_{0}\subseteq\mathfrak{M}\xrightarrow{\sim}\mu_{0}\mathbb{B}^{n}_{x}
such that

•

F_{0}(p)=0
.

•

((F_{0})_{*}\lambda^{i})\big|_{0}=dx^{i}\big|_{0}
for
i=1,\dots,n
.

Take
c>0
be the small constant in Theorem
5.1
. By Proposition
5.15
with
\tilde{c}=c
, we can find a
\kappa_{0}\in(0,\mu_{0}]
and 1-forms
\tilde{\lambda}^{1},\dots,\tilde{\lambda}^{n}\in\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}(\mathbb{B}^{n};T^{*}\mathbb{B}^{n})
such that for scaling map
\phi_{\kappa_{0}}:\mathbb{B}^{n}\to\mu_{0}\mathbb{B}^{n}
,
\phi_{\kappa_{0}}(x)=\kappa_{0}x
, we have

(a)

\tilde{\lambda}^{1},\dots,\tilde{\lambda}^{n}
span the cotangent space at every point.

(b)

\operatorname{supp}(\tilde{\lambda}^{i}-dx^{i})\subsetneq\frac{1}{2}\mathbb{B}^{n}
for
i=1,\dots,n
.

(c)

(\tilde{\lambda}^{1},\dots,\tilde{\lambda}^{n})\big|_{\frac{1}{3}\mathbb{B}^{n}}=\frac{1}{\kappa_{0}}\cdot((F_{0}^{-1}\circ\phi_{\kappa_{0}})^{*}\lambda^{1},\dots,(F_{0}^{-1}\circ\phi_{\kappa_{0}})^{*}\lambda^{n})\big|_{\frac{1}{3}\mathbb{B}^{n}}
.

(d)

\sum_{i=1}^{n}\|\tilde{\lambda}^{i}-dx^{i}\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}}+\|d\tilde{\lambda}^{i}\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\beta-1}}<c
.

We set
F_{1}:=\phi_{\kappa_{0}}^{-1}
.

Applying Theorem
5.1
to
\tilde{\lambda}^{1},\dots,\tilde{\lambda}^{n}
we obtain a
\mathscr{C}^{\alpha+1}
-chart
F_{2}:\mathbb{B}^{n}\xrightarrow{\sim}\mathbb{B}^{n}
such that
F_{2}(\frac{1}{3}\mathbb{B}^{n})\supseteq B^{n}(F_{2}(0),\frac{1}{6})
,
\|F_{2}-\mathrm{id}\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\alpha+1}}<c
and
(F_{2})_{*}\tilde{\lambda}^{1},\dots,(F_{2})_{*}\tilde{\lambda}^{n}\in\mathscr{C}^{\beta}(\mathbb{B}^{n};T^{*}\mathbb{B}^{n})
.

By
(c)
,
\left((F_{2}\circ F_{1}\circ F_{0})_{*}\lambda^{i}\right)\big|_{F_{2}(\frac{1}{3}\mathbb{B}^{n})}={\tfrac{1}{\kappa_{0}}}\cdot(F_{2})_{*}\tilde{\lambda}^{i}\big|_{F_{2}(\frac{1}{3}\mathbb{B}^{n})}\in\mathscr{C}^{\beta}
for
i=1,\dots,n
. We can take an
affine linear transformation

F_{3}:\mathbb{R}^{n}\to\mathbb{R}^{n}
such that
F_{3}(F_{2}(0))=0
and
F_{3}(B^{n}(F_{2}(0),\frac{1}{6}))\supseteq\mathbb{B}^{n}
.

Now we have
F_{0}(U_{0})\supseteq\mu_{0}\mathbb{B}^{n}
,
F_{1}(\mu_{0}\mathbb{B}^{n})\supseteq\mathbb{B}^{n}
,
F_{2}(\frac{1}{3}\mathbb{B}^{n})\supseteq B^{n}(F_{2}(0),\frac{1}{6})
and
F_{3}(B^{n}(F_{2}(0),\frac{1}{6})\supseteq\mathbb{B}^{n}
. Take
\Phi:=(F_{3}\circ F_{2}\circ F_{1}\circ F_{0})^{-1}:\mathbb{B}^{n}\to\mathfrak{M}
. Since
F_{0},F_{1},F_{2},F_{3}
are all
\mathscr{C}^{\alpha+1}
-diffeomorphism onto their images, we know
\Phi:\mathbb{B}^{n}\xrightarrow{\sim}\Phi(\mathbb{B}^{n})\subseteq\mathfrak{M}
is a
\mathscr{C}^{\alpha+1}
diffeomorphism. Moreover, we have
\Phi(0)=p
because
F_{0}(p)=0
,
F_{1}(0)=0
,
F_{3}(F_{2}(0))=0
. Thus,
\Phi
is the diffeomorphism we desire with
U:=\Phi(\mathbb{B}^{n})
, completing the proof.
∎

6
Function Spaces Along Vector Fields, Revisited

Let
\alpha>0
,
\beta>1-\alpha
, and let
\Phi:\mathfrak{N}\xrightarrow{\sim}\mathfrak{M}
be a
\mathscr{C}^{\alpha+1}
-diffeomorphism between two
\mathscr{C}^{\alpha+1}
-manifolds
\mathfrak{N}
and
\mathfrak{M}
. Let
X\in\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}_{\mathrm{loc}}(\mathfrak{M};T\mathfrak{M})
,
f\in\mathscr{C}^{\beta}_{\mathrm{loc}}(\mathfrak{M})
,
Y\in\mathscr{C}^{\beta}_{\mathrm{loc}}(\mathfrak{M};T\mathfrak{M})
and
\theta\in\mathscr{C}^{\beta}_{\mathrm{loc}}\left(\mathfrak{M};{\textstyle\bigwedge}^{k}T^{*}\mathfrak{M}\right)
. We have the following

•

\Phi^{*}(Xf)=(\Phi^{*}X)(\Phi^{*}f)
on
\mathfrak{N}
.

•

\Phi^{*}[X,Y]=[\Phi^{*}X,\Phi^{*}Y]
and
\Phi^{*}(\mathrm{Lie}_{X}\theta)=\mathrm{Lie}_{\Phi^{*}X}\Phi^{*}\theta
on
\mathfrak{N}
, provided that
\alpha>\frac{1}{2}
.

In fact Lemma
4.4
establishes the above facts on open subsets in
\mathbb{R}^{n}
. And since these results are local, they hold in manifolds as well.

Remark 6.1
.

Let
\alpha,\gamma>0
,
\beta\in(-\min(\alpha,\gamma),\infty)
, and let
\mathfrak{M}
and
\mathfrak{N}
be two
\mathscr{C}^{\max(\alpha,\gamma)+1}
-manifolds. Assume
\Phi:\mathfrak{N}\to\mathfrak{M}
is a
\mathscr{C}^{\gamma+1}
-diffeomorphism.
Let
X_{1},\dots,X_{q}
be
\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}_{\mathrm{loc}}
-vector fields on
\mathfrak{M}
that span the tangent space at every point. We write
\Phi^{*}X
for the list of pullback vector fields
(\Phi^{*}X_{1},\dots,\Phi^{*}X_{q})
defined on
\mathfrak{N}
. The following properties follow easily from Definitions
2.13
,
2.14
,
2.15
and
2.16
:

(i)

If
f\in\mathscr{C}^{\beta}_{X,\mathrm{loc}}(\mathfrak{M})
, then
\Phi^{*}f\in\mathscr{C}^{\beta}_{\Phi^{*}X,\mathrm{loc}}(\mathfrak{N})
.

(ii)

If
Y\in\mathscr{C}^{\beta}_{X,\mathrm{loc}}(\mathfrak{M};T\mathfrak{M})
, then
\Phi^{*}Y\in\mathscr{C}^{\beta}_{\Phi^{*}X,\mathrm{loc}}(\mathfrak{N};T\mathfrak{N})
, provided that
\alpha,\gamma>\frac{1}{2}
.

(iii)

Let
1\leq k\leq n
. If
\theta\in\mathscr{C}^{\beta}_{X,\mathrm{loc}}\left(\mathfrak{M};{\textstyle\bigwedge}^{k}T^{*}\mathfrak{M}\right)
, then
\Phi^{*}\theta\in\mathscr{C}^{\beta}_{\Phi^{*}X,\mathrm{loc}}\left(\mathfrak{N};{\textstyle\bigwedge}^{k}T^{*}\mathfrak{N}\right)
, provided that
\alpha,\gamma>\frac{1}{2}
.

(iv)

Let
1\leq k\leq n-1
and let
\theta\in\mathscr{C}^{(-\alpha)^{+}}_{\mathrm{loc}}\left(\mathfrak{M};{\textstyle\bigwedge}^{k}T^{*}\mathfrak{M}\right)
(see Convention
2.12
). If
d\theta
has regularity in
\mathscr{C}^{\beta-1}_{X,\mathrm{loc}}(\mathfrak{M})
, then
d\Phi^{*}\theta
has regularity in
\mathscr{C}^{\beta-1}_{\Phi^{*}X,\mathrm{loc}}(\mathfrak{N})
.

When the vector fields

X_{1},\ldots,X_{q}
are sufficiently smooth, our
\mathscr{C}^{\beta}_{X,\mathrm{loc}}
-spaces coincide with the standard
\mathscr{C}^{\beta}_{\mathrm{loc}}
-spaces, as the next result shows.

Proposition 6.2
.

Let
\alpha>0
and let
X_{1},\dots,X_{q}
be
\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}_{\mathrm{loc}}
-vector fields on a
\mathscr{C}^{\alpha+1}
-manifold
\mathfrak{M}
that span the tangent space at every point. Then

(i)

\mathscr{C}^{\beta}_{X,\mathrm{loc}}(\mathfrak{M})=\mathscr{C}^{\beta}_{\mathrm{loc}}(\mathfrak{M})
for all
\beta\in(-\alpha,\alpha+1]
.

(ii)

When
\alpha>\frac{1}{2}
,
\mathscr{C}^{\beta}_{X,\mathrm{loc}}(\mathfrak{M};T\mathfrak{M})=\mathscr{C}^{\beta}_{\mathrm{loc}}(\mathfrak{M};T\mathfrak{M})
for all
\beta\in(-\alpha,\alpha]
.

(iii)

When
\alpha>\frac{1}{2}
, for
1\leq k\leq n
,
\mathscr{C}^{\beta}_{X,\mathrm{loc}}\left(\mathfrak{M};{\textstyle\bigwedge}^{k}T^{*}\mathfrak{M}\right)=\mathscr{C}^{\beta}_{\mathrm{loc}}\left(\mathfrak{M};{\textstyle\bigwedge}^{k}T^{*}\mathfrak{M}\right)
for all
\beta\in(-\alpha,\alpha]
.

(iv)

Assume
\alpha\geq 1
. Let
\beta\in(1-\alpha,1+\alpha]
and
1\leq k\leq n
. Let
\theta\in\mathscr{C}^{(-\alpha)^{+}}_{\mathrm{loc}}\left(\mathfrak{M};{\textstyle\bigwedge}^{k}T^{*}\mathfrak{M}\right)
be a
k
-form, then
d\theta
has regularity
\mathscr{C}^{\beta-1}_{X,\mathrm{loc}}(\mathfrak{M})
if and only if
d\theta\in\mathscr{C}^{\beta-1}_{\mathrm{loc}}\left(\mathfrak{M};{\textstyle\bigwedge}^{k+1}T^{*}\mathfrak{M}\right)
.

To prove Proposition
6.2
we need a simple result concerning Hölder-Zygmund spaces:

Lemma 6.3
.

Let
U\subseteq\mathbb{R}^{n}
be an open subset, let
\beta\in\mathbb{R}
and
f\in\mathscr{D}^{\prime}(U)
. Then
f\in\mathscr{C}^{\beta}_{\mathrm{loc}}(U)
if and only if
f,\partial_{x^{1}}f,\dots,\partial_{x^{n}}f\in\mathscr{C}^{\beta-1}_{\mathrm{loc}}(U)
.

Proof.

When
\beta>1
, the result follows from Remark
2.1

(iii)
. Thus,
we consider only the case
\beta\leq 1
.

Let
V\Subset U
