u\in\mathscr{C}^{s+1}(\mathbb{B}^{n};\mathbb{R}^{n^{2}})
with
\|u\|_{\mathscr{C}^{s+1}}\lesssim_{n,s_{0},s,\|\eta^{i}\|_{\mathscr{C}^{s}},\|d\eta^{i}\|_{\mathscr{C}^{s}}}\|u\|_{\mathscr{C}^{s_{0}+1}}+\|g_{B}\|_{\mathscr{C}^{s_{0}}}
.

Thus, for any
\tilde{\sigma}>0
there is
C^{\prime}_{0}=C^{\prime}_{0}(n,s_{0},s,c_{3},\gamma,\tilde{\sigma})>0
that does not depend on
B
, such that

\begin{gathered}\text{ if }\sum_{i,j,k,l=1}^{n}\|\widetilde{\mathcal{R}}_{il}^{jk}(B)\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{B}^{n})}<\gamma\quad\text{and}\quad\sum_{i=1}^{n}\|\eta^{i}-dy^{i}\|_{\mathscr{C}^{s}(\mathbb{B}^{n};T^{*}\mathbb{B}^{n})}+\|d\eta^{i}\|_{\mathscr{C}^{s}(\mathbb{B}^{n};\wedge^{2}T^{*}\mathbb{B}^{n})}<\tilde{\sigma},\\
\text{ then }\|B\|_{\mathscr{C}^{s+1}(\frac{3}{4}\mathbb{B}^{n};\mathbb{M}^{n\times n})}\leq C^{\prime}_{0}(n,s_{0},s,c_{3},\gamma,\tilde{\sigma}).\end{gathered}

(9.14)

When
\|B\|_{L^{\infty}}
suitably small we have

\sum_{i,j,k,l=1}^{n}\|\widetilde{\mathcal{R}}_{il}^{jk}(B)\|_{L^{\infty}}\lesssim_{\mathcal{R}}\|B\|_{L^{\infty}}\lesssim\sum_{k=1}^{n}\|\eta^{k}-dy^{k}\|_{L^{\infty}}\lesssim_{s_{0}}\sum_{k=1}^{n}\|\eta^{k}-dy^{k}\|_{\mathscr{C}^{s_{0}}}.

So we can take a
c_{3}^{\prime}\in(0,c_{3})
(which still only depends on
n,s_{0}
) such that

\sum_{k=1}^{n}\|\eta^{k}-dy^{k}\|_{\mathscr{C}^{s_{0}}}+\|d\eta^{k}\|_{\mathscr{C}^{s_{0}}}<c_{3}^{\prime}\quad\Rightarrow\quad\sum_{i,j,k,l=1}^{n}\|\widetilde{\mathcal{R}}_{il}^{jk}(B)\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{B}^{n})}<\gamma.

(9.15)

Using the same proof as Theorem
5.1
in Section
5.4
, take
c^{\prime}=\frac{1}{2n^{2}}\min(c_{1},c_{2}c_{3}^{\prime})
and we see that
\sum_{k=1}^{n}\|\lambda^{k}-dx^{k}\|_{\mathscr{C}^{s_{0}}}+\|d\lambda^{k}\|_{\mathscr{C}^{s_{0}}}<c^{\prime}
implies
\sum_{k=1}^{n}\|\eta^{k}-dy^{k}\|_{\mathscr{C}^{s_{0}}}+\|d\eta^{k}\|_{\mathscr{C}^{s_{0}}}<c_{3}^{\prime}
and
\|F-\mathrm{id}\|_{\mathscr{C}^{s_{0}+1}(\mathbb{B}^{n};\mathbb{R}^{n})}+\sum_{i=1}^{n}\|\eta^{i}-dy^{i}\|_{\mathscr{C}^{s_{0}+1}(\mathbb{B}^{n};\mathbb{R}^{n})}\leq 1
.

We then prove
(ii)
and
(iii)
using this constant
c^{\prime}
.

(ii)
: Recall by assumption
\operatorname{supp}A\subsetneq\frac{1}{2}\mathbb{B}^{n}
, so
\bigtriangleup R\big|_{\mathbb{B}^{n}\backslash\frac{1}{2}\mathbb{B}^{n}}=0
and thus similar to (
5.18
) we have

\|R\|_{\mathscr{C}^{s+1}(\mathbb{B}^{n}\backslash\frac{3}{4}\mathbb{B}^{n};\mathbb{R}^{n})}\lesssim_{s,s_{0}}\|R\|_{\mathscr{C}^{s_{0}+1}(\mathbb{B}^{n};\mathbb{R}^{n})}\lesssim_{s_{0}}\|A\|_{\mathscr{C}^{s_{0}}}.

(9.16)

On the other hand by classical interior Schauder estimates we know that

\|R\|_{\mathscr{C}^{s+1}(\frac{4}{5}\mathbb{B}^{n})}\leq C\sum_{i,j,k=1}^{n}\|\sqrt{\det g}g^{ij}a_{i}^{k}\|_{\mathscr{C}^{s}(\mathbb{B}^{n})},

(9.17)

where
C
is a constant that only depends on
n,s
, the upper bounds of
\|I+A\|_{C^{0}(\mathbb{B}^{n};\mathbb{M}^{n\times n})},\|(I+A)^{-1}\|_{C^{0}(\mathbb{B}^{n};\mathbb{M}^{n\times n})}
and
\|\sqrt{\det g}g^{ij}\|_{\mathscr{C}^{s}(\mathbb{B}^{n})}
. For the precise form of the interior Schauder’s estimate we use, see, for example,
[
FRRO20
, Corollary 2.28]
for
s>1,s\notin\mathbb{Z}
and
[
GT01
, Theorem 8.32]
for
0<s<1
. The proof for
s\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}
is similar to these.

In Proposition
5.10
we chose
c_{1}
small so that
\|A\|_{C^{0}}<\frac{1}{2}
; thus
\|I+A\|_{C^{0}(\mathbb{B}^{n};\mathbb{M}^{n\times n})}
and
\|(I+A)^{-1}\|_{C^{0}(\mathbb{B}^{n};\mathbb{M}^{n\times n})}
are already uniformly bounded. And since
\sqrt{\det g}g^{ij}a_{i}^{k}
and
\sqrt{\det g}g^{ij}
are all polynomials of the components of
A
, by Lemma
2.2
their
\mathscr{C}^{s}
-norms are bounded by a constant depending only on the upper bound of
\|A\|_{\mathscr{C}^{s}}
.
Therefore combining (
9.16
) and (
9.17
), since
F=\mathrm{id}+R
, we have

\|F\|_{\mathscr{C}^{s+1}(\mathbb{B}^{n})}\leq C_{1}^{\prime}(n,s,s_{0},c_{1},M^{\prime}),

(9.18)

for some
C_{1}^{\prime}>0
that only depends on
n,s,s_{0}
and an upper bound for
\|A\|_{\mathscr{C}^{s}}
. Since
\|A\|_{\mathscr{C}^{s}}\leq M^{\prime}
,
C_{1}^{\prime}
does not
depend on
\|A\|_{\mathscr{C}^{s}}
, just on
M^{\prime}
.

By Proposition
5.10

(iii)
, we have
\Phi=F^{-1}:\mathbb{B}^{n}\xrightarrow{\sim}\mathbb{B}^{n}
and
\|\Phi\|_{\mathscr{C}^{s_{0}+1}(\mathbb{B}^{n};\mathbb{R}^{n})}<c_{1}^{-1}
, where
c_{1}=c_{1}(n,s_{0},s_{0}+1)
is the constant from Proposition
5.10
. So
\inf\limits_{x\in\mathbb{B}^{n}}|\det(\nabla F)(x)|
is bounded below by a constant depending only on
n,s_{0},c_{1}
. Applying
[
Str21
, Lemmas 5.9 and 5.8]
with
\|F\|_{\mathscr{C}^{s+1}(\mathbb{B}^{n})}\leq C_{1}^{\prime}
and
\|A\|_{\mathscr{C}^{s}(\mathbb{B}^{n};\mathbb{M}^{n\times n})}+\sum_{i=1}^{n}\|d\lambda^{i}\|_{\mathscr{C}^{s}}<M^{\prime}
we get
\Phi\in\mathscr{C}^{s+1}(\mathbb{B}^{n};\mathbb{R}^{n})
with

\|\Phi\|_{\mathscr{C}^{s+1}(\mathbb{B}^{n};\mathbb{R}^{n})}+\|A\circ\Phi\|_{\mathscr{C}^{s}(\mathbb{B}^{n};\mathbb{M}^{n\times n})}+\sum_{i,j,k=1}^{n}\left\|\left(d\lambda^{i}\big(\tfrac{\partial}{\partial x^{j}},\tfrac{\partial}{\partial x^{k}}\big)\right)\circ\Phi\right\|_{\mathscr{C}^{s}(\mathbb{B}^{n})}\leq C_{2}^{\prime},

(9.19)

for some
C_{2}^{\prime}>0
that only depends on
n,s,s_{0},C_{1}^{\prime}
and the upper bound of
\|A\|_{\mathscr{C}^{s}}
. Since
\|A\|_{\mathscr{C}^{s}}\leq M^{\prime}
and
C_{1}^{\prime}
depends only
on
n,s,s_{0},c_{1}
and
M^{\prime}
, the same is true of
C_{2}^{\prime}
, i.e.

C_{2}^{\prime}=C_{2}^{\prime}(n,s,s_{0},c_{1},M^{\prime})
. Taking
\zeta
such that
\zeta^{-1}\geq C_{2}^{\prime}
we complete the proof of
(ii)
.

(iii)
: By a direct computation (also see (
5.20
)) we have

B=\nabla\Phi-I+(A\circ\Phi)\nabla\Phi,\quad d\eta^{i}=\Phi^{*}d\lambda^{i}=\sum_{1\leq j<k\leq n}\left(\left(d\lambda^{i}\big(\tfrac{\partial}{\partial x^{j}},\tfrac{\partial}{\partial x^{k}}\big)\right)\circ\Phi\right)\cdot d\phi^{j}\wedge d\phi^{k}.

(9.20)

Applying Lemma
2.2
to (
9.20
) and using (
9.19
) we can find a
C^{\prime}_{3}=C^{\prime}_{3}(n,s,s_{0},c_{1},M^{\prime})
such that,

\|B\|_{\mathscr{C}^{s}(\mathbb{B}^{n};\mathbb{M}^{n\times n})}+\sum_{i=1}^{n}\|d\eta^{i}\|_{\mathscr{C}^{s}(\mathbb{B}^{n};\wedge^{2}T^{*}\mathbb{B}^{n})}<C^{\prime}_{3}(n,s,s_{0},c_{1},M^{\prime}).

(9.21)

Applying (
9.14
) with
\tilde{\sigma}=C^{\prime}_{3}
, where we recall that
\gamma
depends only on
n
, we see that
C^{\prime}_{0}(n,s_{0},s,c_{3}^{\prime},\gamma,C^{\prime}_{3})
is a constant depending only on
n,s_{0},s,M^{\prime}
.

Take
K^{\prime}=C_{2}^{\prime}(n,s,s_{0},c_{1},M^{\prime})+C^{\prime}_{0}(n,s,s_{0},c_{3}^{\prime},\gamma,C^{\prime}_{3})
, since
c_{1}
and
c_{3}^{\prime}
are constants that only depend on
n,s_{0}
we know
K^{\prime}=K^{\prime}(n,s,s_{0},M^{\prime})
depends only on
n,s,s_{0},M^{\prime}
, which completes the proof of
(iii)
.
∎

The proof of Proposition
5.15
gives a similar regularity estimate:

Lemma 9.7
.

Let
\alpha,\beta\in[\alpha,\alpha+1],\mu_{0},\tilde{c},M>0
be as in Proposition
5.15
. Let
s\geq\alpha
,
\tilde{M}>1
, there is a
\tilde{K}=\tilde{K}(n,\alpha,\beta,s,\mu_{0},\tilde{c},M,\tilde{M})>0
that satisfies the following:

Let
\theta^{1},\dots,\theta^{n}\in\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}(\mu_{0}\mathbb{B}^{n};T^{*}\mathbb{R}^{n})
be as in the assumptions of Proposition
5.15
. Suppose in addition to these assumptions we have
\theta^{1},\dots,\theta^{n}\in\mathscr{C}^{s}
,
d\theta^{1},\dots,d\theta^{n}\in\mathscr{C}^{s}
with estimate

\sum_{i=1}^{n}\|\theta^{i}\|_{\mathscr{C}^{s}(\mu_{0}\mathbb{B}^{n};T^{*}\mathbb{R}^{n})}+\|d\theta^{i}\|_{\mathscr{C}^{s}(\mu_{0}\mathbb{B}^{n};\wedge^{2}T^{*}\mathbb{R}^{n})}<\tilde{M}.

(9.22)

Then
\lambda^{1},\dots,\lambda^{n}
constructed in Proposition
5.15
satisfy
\lambda^{1},\dots,\lambda^{n}\in\mathscr{C}^{s}
,
d\lambda^{1},\dots,d\lambda^{n}\in\mathscr{C}^{s}
with estimate

\sum_{i=1}^{n}\|\lambda^{i}\|_{\mathscr{C}^{s}(\mathbb{B}^{n};T^{*}\mathbb{R}^{n})}+\|d\lambda^{i}\|_{\mathscr{C}^{s}(\mathbb{B}^{n};\wedge^{2}T^{*}\mathbb{R}^{n})}<\tilde{K}.

Proof.

In the proof of Proposition
5.15
, we construct
\lambda^{1},\dots,\lambda^{n}
as follows: For
i=1,\dots,n
,

\displaystyle\tilde{\rho}^{i}:=\mathcal{G}\ast\vartheta(\chi_{0}\cdot d\theta^{i}+d\chi_{0}\wedge\theta^{i}),\quad\rho^{i}:=\tilde{\rho}^{i}-(\tilde{\rho}^{i}\big|_{0}),

(9.23)

\displaystyle\lambda^{i}:=dx^{i}+\tfrac{1}{\kappa_{0}}\chi_{1}\cdot\phi_{\kappa_{0}}^{*}(\theta^{i}-dx^{i}),\quad\tau^{i}:=\tfrac{1}{\kappa_{0}}\chi_{1}\cdot(\phi_{\kappa_{0}}^{*}\rho^{i})+\tfrac{1}{\kappa_{0}}\mathcal{G}\ast\vartheta\left(d\chi_{1}\wedge\phi_{\kappa_{0}}^{*}(\theta^{i}-dx^{i})\right).

(9.24)

Here
\chi_{0}\in C_{c}^{\infty}(\mu_{0}\mathbb{B}^{n})
satisfies
\chi_{0}\big|_{\frac{\mu_{0}}{2}\mathbb{B}^{n}}\equiv 1
;
\chi_{1}\in C_{c}^{\infty}(\frac{1}{2}\mathbb{B}^{n})
satisfies
\chi_{1}\big|_{\frac{1}{3}\mathbb{B}^{n}}\equiv 1
;
\mathcal{G}
is the Newtonian potential;
\vartheta
is the codifferential operator;
\kappa_{0}=\kappa_{0}(n,\alpha,\beta,\mu_{0},\tilde{c},M)>0
is the scaling constant and
\phi_{\kappa_{0}}(x)=\kappa_{0}x
.

By assumption
\theta^{i},d\theta^{i}\in\mathscr{C}^{s}
with bound (
9.22
). Since
\phi_{\kappa_{0}}
is a scaling map depending only on
\kappa_{0}
, we have

\|\lambda^{i}\|_{\mathscr{C}^{s}(\mathbb{B}^{n};T^{*}\mathbb{R}^{n})}\lesssim_{\kappa_{0},\mu_{0},\chi_{1}}1+\|\theta^{i}-dx^{i}\|_{\mathscr{C}^{s}(\mu_{0}\mathbb{B}^{n};T^{*}\mathbb{R}^{n})}\lesssim_{\kappa_{0},\chi_{1}}\tilde{M}\quad i=1,\dots,n.

(9.25)

Applying Lemma
4.7
(see also (
5.49
)), with the same argument as (
5.44
), we have for
i=1,\dots,n
,

\|\rho^{i}\|_{\mathscr{C}^{s+1}(\mu_{0}\mathbb{B}^{n};T^{*}\mathbb{R}^{n})}\lesssim_{s,\mu_{0},\chi_{0}}\tilde{M}.

Also, by a direct estimate,

\|\tau^{i}\|_{\mathscr{C}^{s+1}(\mu_{0}\mathbb{B}^{n};T^{*}\mathbb{R}^{n})}\lesssim_{\kappa_{0},s,\mu_{0},\chi_{1}}\|\rho^{i}\|_{\mathscr{C}^{s+1}}+\|\theta^{i}-dx^{i}\|_{\mathscr{C}^{s}}\lesssim_{s,\kappa_{0},\mu_{0},\chi_{0},\chi_{1}}\tilde{M}.

In (
5.49
) it is shown that
d\lambda^{i}=d\tau^{i}
and therefore,

\|d\lambda^{i}\|_{\mathscr{C}^{s+1}(\mu_{0}\mathbb{B}^{n};\wedge^{2}T^{*}\mathbb{R}^{n})}=\|d\tau^{i}\|_{\mathscr{C}^{s+1}(\mu_{0}\mathbb{B}^{n};\wedge^{2}T^{*}\mathbb{R}^{n})}\lesssim_{s}\|\tau^{i}\|_{\mathscr{C}^{s+1}(\mu_{0}\mathbb{B}^{n};T^{*}\mathbb{R}^{n})}\lesssim_{s,\kappa_{0},\mu_{0},\chi_{0},\chi_{1}}\tilde{M}.

(9.26)

Combining (
9.25
) and (
9.26
), since
\chi_{0}
and
\chi_{1}
are fixed cut-off functions and
\kappa_{0}=\kappa_{0}(n,\alpha,\beta,\mu_{0},\tilde{c},M)
, we get
\tilde{K}=\tilde{K}(n,s,\mu_{0},\kappa_{0},\tilde{M})=\tilde{K}(n,\alpha,\beta,s,\mu_{0},\tilde{c},M,\tilde{M})
as desired.
∎

We can now prove Proposition
9.4
by applying Proposition
9.6
and Lemma
9.7
.

Proof of Proposition
9.4
.

Let
\theta^{1},\dots,\theta^{n}
be the dual basis to
Y_{1},\dots,Y_{n}
on
B^{n}(0,\mu_{0})
. Write
\theta=[\theta^{1},\dots,\theta^{n}]^{\top}
as
\theta=(I+B)dx
where
B=(I+A)^{-1}-I
and
dx=[dx^{1},\dots,dx^{n}]^{\top}
.

Clearly
B(0)=0
because
\nabla\Phi_{0}(0)=I
. So

\|(I+A)^{-1}\|_{C^{0}(B^{n}(0,\mu_{0});\mathbb{M}^{n\times n})}\leq\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}\|A\|_{C^{0}(B^{n}(0,\mu_{0});\mathbb{M}^{n\times n})}^{j}\leq\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}2^{-j}\leq 2,\text{ implying }\inf\limits_{|x|<\mu_{0}}|\det(I+A(x))|\geq 2^{-n}.

(9.27)

By assumption

\|I+A\|_{\mathscr{C}^{s_{0}}(B^{n}(0,\mu_{0});\mathbb{M}^{n\times n})}\leq\|I\|_{\mathscr{C}^{s_{0}}(B^{n}(0,\mu_{0});\mathbb{M}^{n\times n})}+\|A\|_{\mathscr{C}^{s_{0}}(B^{n}(0,\mu_{0});\mathbb{M}^{n\times n})}\lesssim_{n,s_{0},\mu_{0},M_{0}}1
. Applying

[
Str21
, Lemma 5.7]
along with (
9.27
), we have
\|I+B\|_{\mathscr{C}^{s_{0}}(B^{n}(0,\mu_{0});\mathbb{M}^{n\times n})}\lesssim_{n,s_{0},\mu_{0},M_{0}}1
, which means

\exists\widehat{M}_{1}=\widehat{M}_{1}(n,s_{0},\mu_{0},M_{0})>0,\quad\text{such that }\sum_{i=1}^{n}\|\theta^{i}\|_{\mathscr{C}^{s_{0}}(B^{n}(0,\mu_{0});T^{*}\mathbb{R}^{n})}\leq\widehat{M}_{1}.

(9.28)

By (
7.1
),
[X_{i},X_{j}]=\sum_{k=1}^{n}c_{ij}^{k}X_{k}
implies that

d((\Phi_{0})_{*}\theta^{k})=\sum_{1\leq i<j\leq n}c_{ij}^{k}((\Phi_{0})_{*}\theta^{i})\wedge((\Phi_{0})_{*}\theta^{j}),\quad\text{so}\quad d\theta^{k}=\sum_{1\leq i<j\leq n}\tilde{c}_{ij}^{k}\theta^{i}\wedge\theta^{j},\quad k=1,\dots,n.

(9.29)

Note that we cannot say
[Y_{i},Y_{j}]=\sum_{k=1}^{n}\tilde{c}_{ij}^{k}Y_{k}
since we cannot define
[Y_{i},Y_{j}]
when
s\in(0,\frac{1}{2}]
, while
d\theta^{k}
and
\tilde{c}_{ij}^{k}\theta^{i}\wedge\theta^{j}
in (
9.29
) are defined due to Proposition
2.7

(ii)
with the equality holding in the sense of distributions.

So by Lemma
2.2
we have

\sum_{k=1}^{n}\|d\theta^{k}\|_{\mathscr{C}^{s_{0}}(B^{n}(0,\mu_{0});T^{*}\mathbb{R}^{n})}\lesssim_{s_{0},\mu_{0}}\sum_{i,j,k=1}^{n}\|\tilde{c}_{ij}^{k}\|_{\mathscr{C}^{s_{0}}}\|\theta^{i}\|_{\mathscr{C}^{s_{0}}}\|\theta^{j}\|_{\mathscr{C}^{s_{0}}}\lesssim_{n,s_{0},\mu_{0}}M_{0}^{2}\widehat{M}_{1}^{2}\lesssim_{n,s_{0},\mu_{0},M_{0}}1.

(9.30)

In other words,

\exists\widehat{M}_{2}=\widehat{M}_{2}(n,s_{0},\mu_{0},M_{0})>0,\quad\text{such that }\sum_{i=1}^{n}\|d\theta^{i}\|_{\mathscr{C}^{s_{0}}(B^{n}(0,\mu_{0});T^{*}\mathbb{R}^{n})}\leq\widehat{M}_{2}.

(9.31)

Applying Lemma
9.7

with
\alpha=s_{0}
,
\beta=s_{0}+1
,
M=\widehat{M}_{1}+\widehat{M}_{2}
,
\mu_{0}=\mu_{0}
and
\tilde{c}=c^{\prime}
, where
\widehat{M}_{1}
is in (
9.28
),
\widehat{M}_{2}
is in (
9.31
) and
c^{\prime}=c^{\prime}(n,s_{0})
is the constant in Proposition
9.6
, we can find
\kappa_{0}=\kappa_{0}(n,s_{0},\mu_{0},M_{0})\in(0,\mu_{0}]
and 1-forms
\lambda^{1},\dots,\lambda^{n}\in\mathscr{C}^{s_{0}}(\mathbb{B}^{n};T^{*}\mathbb{B}^{n})
that satisfy the assumptions
of Proposition
9.6
with constant
c^{\prime}
, that is

(a)

\lambda^{1},\dots,\lambda^{n}
span the tangent space at every point in
\mathbb{B}^{n}
.

(b)

\operatorname{supp}(\lambda^{i}-dx^{i})\Subset\frac{1}{2}\mathbb{B}^{n}
for
i=1,\dots,n
.

(c)

\lambda^{i}\big|_{\frac{1}{3}\mathbb{B}^{n}}=\frac{1}{\kappa_{0}}\cdot(\phi_{\kappa_{0}}^{*}\theta^{i})\big|_{\frac{1}{3}\mathbb{B}^{n}}
for
i=1,\dots,n
. Here
\phi_{\kappa_{0}}:\mathbb{B}^{n}\to B^{n}(0,\mu_{0})
,
\phi_{\kappa_{0}}(x)=\kappa_{0}\cdot x
.

(d)

\sum_{i=1}^{n}(\|\lambda^{i}-dx^{i}\|_{\mathscr{C}^{s_{0}}}+\|d\lambda^{i}\|_{\mathscr{C}^{s_{0}}})\leq c^{\prime}

By Proposition
9.6
with this
c^{\prime}
(see Theorem
5.1
, with
\alpha=s_{0}
and
\beta=s_{0}+1
), we can find a map
F:\mathbb{B}^{n}\xrightarrow{\sim}\mathbb{B}^{n}
, such that
F(\frac{1}{3}\mathbb{B}^{n})\supseteq B^{n}(F(0),\frac{1}{6})
and by endowing the codomain of
F
with standard coordinate system
y=(y^{1},\dots,y^{n})
,

\|F-\mathrm{id}\|_{\mathscr{C}^{s_{0}+1}(\mathbb{B}^{n};\mathbb{R}^{n})}+\|F_{*}\lambda-dy\|_{\mathscr{C}^{s_{0}+1}(\mathbb{B}^{n};\mathbb{M}^{n\times n})}\leq c^{\prime-1}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left(\|\lambda^{i}-dy^{i}\|_{\mathscr{C}^{s_{0}}(\mathbb{B}^{n};\mathbb{R}^{n})}+\|d\lambda^{i}\|_{\mathscr{C}^{s_{0}}(\mathbb{B}^{n};\wedge^{2}T^{*}\mathbb{B}^{n})}\right).

(9.32)

Write
F_{*}\lambda=:(I+\widehat{B})dy
. Note that by condition
(d)
, the right hand side of (
9.32
) is bounded by
1
and therefore
\|F_{*}\lambda-dy\|_{\mathscr{C}^{s_{0}+1}(\mathbb{B}^{n};\mathbb{M}^{n\times n})}\leq 1
, and
there is a
C_{1}=C_{1}(n,s_{0})>0
such that

\|I+\widehat{B}\|_{\mathscr{C}^{s_{0}+1}(\mathbb{B}^{n};\mathbb{M}^{n\times n})}\leq C_{1}(n,s_{0}).

(9.33)

And by Proposition
9.6

(i)
we have
\|\widehat{B}\|_{C^{0}(\mathbb{B}^{n};\mathbb{M}^{n\times n})}<\frac{1}{4}
. So

\textstyle|(I+\widehat{B}(F(0)))^{-1}|_{\mathbb{M}^{n\times n}}\leq\|(I+\widehat{B})^{-1}\|_{C^{0}(\mathbb{B}^{n};\mathbb{M}^{n\times n})}\leq\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}(1/4)^{k}=\frac{4}{3}.

(9.34)

Define an affine linear map

\psi(t):=(I+\widehat{B}(F(0)))^{-1}\cdot\tfrac{t}{9}+F(0),\quad t\in\mathbb{B}^{n}.

(9.35)

Note that by (
9.34
) we have
\psi(\mathbb{B}^{n})\subseteq\frac{4}{3}\cdot\frac{1}{9}\mathbb{B}^{n}+F(0)\subset B^{n}(F(0),\frac{1}{6})
.

Define
\Phi_{1}:\mathbb{B}^{n}\to B^{n}(0,\mu_{0})
by

\Phi_{1}(t):=\phi_{\kappa_{0}}\circ F^{-1}\circ\psi(t)=\kappa_{0}\cdot F^{-1}\left((I+\widehat{B}(F(0)))^{-1}\cdot\tfrac{t}{9}+F(0)\right),\quad t\in\mathbb{B}^{n}.

(9.36)

Here
\Phi_{1}
is well-defined because
\psi(\mathbb{B}^{n})\subset B^{n}(F(0),\frac{1}{6})
,
B^{n}(F(0),\frac{1}{6})\subseteq F(\frac{1}{3}\mathbb{B}^{n})
and
\phi_{\kappa_{0}}(\frac{1}{3}\mathbb{B}^{n})\subset\mu_{0}\mathbb{B}^{n}
. Clearly
\Phi_{1}(0)=\kappa_{0}\cdot F^{-1}(F(0))=0
.

By condition
(c)
,
\lambda^{i}\big|_{\frac{1}{3}\mathbb{B}^{n}}=\frac{1}{\kappa_{0}}\cdot(\phi_{\kappa_{0}}^{*}\theta^{i})\big|_{\frac{1}{3}\mathbb{B}^{n}}
, and the fact that
F^{-1}\circ\psi(\mathbb{B}^{n})\subset\frac{1}{3}\mathbb{B}^{n}
, we have

\displaystyle(\Phi_{1}^{*}\theta)(t)

\displaystyle=\kappa_{0}\psi^{*}(F_{*}\lambda)=\kappa_{0}\psi^{*}((I+\widehat{B}(y))dy)=\kappa_{0}\left(I+\widehat{B}(\psi(t))\right)d\psi(t)

(9.37)

\displaystyle=\tfrac{\kappa_{0}}{9}\left(I+\widehat{B}(\psi(t))\right)\cdot\left(I+\widehat{B}(F(0))\right)^{-1}dt.

Since
\Phi_{1}^{*}Y_{1},\dots,\Phi_{1}^{*}Y_{n}
and
\Phi_{1}^{*}\theta^{1},\dots,\Phi_{1}^{*}\theta^{n}
are dual basis to each other, we can write

\Phi_{1}^{*}Y=:\tfrac{9}{\kappa_{0}}\cdot(I+\widehat{A})\tfrac{\partial}{\partial t},\quad\text{where }\widehat{A}(t)=(I+\widehat{B}(F(0)))\cdot(I+\widehat{B}(\psi(t)))^{-1}-I,\quad t\in\mathbb{B}^{n}.

(9.38)

Taking
t=0
in (
9.38
), since
\psi(0)=F(0)
, we get
\widehat{A}(0)=0
.

Let
\widehat{K}:=\frac{9}{\kappa_{0}}
, since
\kappa_{0}=\kappa_{0}(n,s_{0},\mu_{0},M_{0})
, we have
\widehat{K}=\widehat{K}(n,s_{0},\mu_{0},M_{0})
is as desired for
(i)
.

Since
\|\widehat{B}\|_{C^{0}}<\frac{1}{4}
and using the power series
\widehat{A}(t)=(I+\widehat{B}(F(0)))\cdot\sum_{j=1}^{\infty}\widehat{B}(\psi(t))^{j}
, we have

\|\widehat{A}\|_{C^{0}(\mathbb{B}^{n};\mathbb{M}^{n\times n})}\leq|(I+\widehat{B}(F(0)))|_{\mathbb{M}^{n\times n}}\sum_{j=1}^{\infty}\|\widehat{B}\|_{C^{0}}^{j}<\tfrac{5}{4}\sum_{j=1}^{\infty}(\tfrac{1}{4})^{j}=\tfrac{5}{3}<\frac{1}{2},\quad\|I+\widehat{A}\|_{C^{0}(\mathbb{B}^{n};\mathbb{M}^{n\times n})}<\frac{3}{2}.

(9.39)

This finishes the proof of (
9.8
).

To prove (
9.9
) we need to find the constant
K_{0}
.

Applying
[
Str21
, Lemma 5.7]
to (
9.39
), (
9.38
), and (
9.33
), we see that
there is a
C_{2}=C_{2}(n,s_{0},C_{1})=C_{2}(n,s_{0},\mu_{0},M_{0})>0
such that

\|I+\widehat{A}\|_{\mathscr{C}^{s_{0}+1}(\mathbb{B}^{n};\mathbb{M}^{n\times n})}\leq C_{2}(n,s_{0},\mu_{0},M_{0}).

(9.40)

Since
F
is constructed in Proposition
5.10
(see Remark
5.12
), by Proposition
5.10

(iii)
we have
\|F^{-1}\|_{\mathscr{C}^{s_{0}+1}}\leq c_{1}^{-1}
where
c_{1}=c_{1}(n,s_{0},s_{0}+1)
is the constant in Proposition
5.10
. Since by (
9.36
),
\Phi_{1}
is an affine transform of
F^{-1}
, we have
\|\Phi_{1}\|_{\mathscr{C}^{s_{0}+1}(\mathbb{B}^{n};\mathbb{R}^{n})}\lesssim_{n,s_{0},\kappa_{0}}\|F^{-1}\|_{\mathscr{C}^{s_{0}+1}(\mathbb{B}^{n};\mathbb{R}^{n})}
. So we can find a
C_{3}=C_{3}(n,s_{0},\mu_{0})>0
such that
\|\Phi_{1}\|_{\mathscr{C}^{s_{0}+1}(\mathbb{B}^{n};\mathbb{R}^{n})}\leq C_{3}
.

Taking
K_{0}=\max(C_{2}+\|I\|_{\mathscr{C}^{s_{0}+1}(\mathbb{B}^{n};\mathbb{M}^{n\times n})},C_{3})
, we get (
9.9
) which completes the proof of
(i)
.

We now focus on the proof of
(ii)
, where we assume that additionally we have (
9.10
). By
[
Str21
, Lemma 5.7]
along with (
9.27
), we have
\|(I+A)^{-1}\|_{\mathscr{C}^{s}}\lesssim_{n,s,\mu_{0},M_{0},M_{1}}1
, i.e.
\|\theta^{i}\|_{\mathscr{C}^{s}}\lesssim_{n,s,\mu_{0}}1
. By (
9.29
) with the same argument as (
9.30
), we get
\|d\theta^{i}\|_{\mathscr{C}^{s}}\lesssim_{n,s,\mu_{0},M_{0},M_{1}}1
. In other words, where exists a
\tilde{M}=\tilde{M}(n,s,\mu_{0},M_{0},M_{1})>0
such that

\sum_{i=1}^{n}\big(\|\theta^{i}\|_{\mathscr{C}^{s}(B^{n}(0,\mu_{0}),T^{*}\mathbb{R}^{n})}+\|d\theta^{i}\|_{\mathscr{C}^{s}(B^{n}(0,\mu_{0}),\wedge^{2}T^{*}\mathbb{R}^{n})}\big)\leq\tilde{M}.

By Lemma
9.7
, the 1-forms
\lambda^{1},\dots,\lambda^{n}\in\mathscr{C}^{s_{0}}(\mathbb{B}^{n};T^{*}\mathbb{B}^{n})
constructed above are all
\mathscr{C}^{s}
and satisfy
d\lambda^{1},\dots,d\lambda^{n}\in\mathscr{C}^{s}
with estimate

\sum_{i=1}^{n}\big(\|\lambda^{i}\|_{\mathscr{C}^{s}(\mathbb{B},T^{*}\mathbb{R}^{n})}+\|d\lambda^{i}\|_{\mathscr{C}^{s}(\mathbb{B}^{n},\wedge^{2}T^{*}\mathbb{R}^{n})}\big)\leq\tilde{K}(n,s,s_{0},\mu_{0},M_{0},M_{1}),

(9.41)

where
\tilde{K}=\tilde{K}(n,s,s_{0},\mu_{0},M_{0},M_{1})>0
is the constant obtained in Lemma
9.7
.

By Proposition
9.6
with assumption (
9.41
) (i.e.
M^{\prime}=\tilde{K}
in its assumption), we have
F\in\mathscr{C}^{s+1}(\mathbb{B}^{n};\mathbb{R}^{n})
,
F_{*}\lambda^{1},\dots,F_{*}\lambda^{n}\in\mathscr{C}^{s+1}
, and moreover there is a
C_{4}=C_{4}(n,s_{0},s,\mu_{0},M_{0},M_{1})>0
(which is the
K^{\prime}
in the conclusion of Proposition
9.6
) that does not depend on
F
and
\lambda^{1},\dots,\lambda^{n}
, such that

\|F^{-1}\|_{\mathscr{C}^{s+1}(\mathbb{B}^{n};\mathbb{R}^{n})}+\|F_{*}\lambda\|_{\mathscr{C}^{s+1}(\frac{3}{4}\mathbb{B}^{n};\mathbb{M}^{n\times n})}\leq C_{4}(n,s_{0},s,\mu_{0},M_{0},M_{1}).

(9.42)

Since we have
B^{n}(F(0),\frac{1}{6})\subseteq F(\frac{1}{3}\mathbb{B}^{n})\cap\frac{3}{4}\mathbb{B}^{n}
from Theorem
5.1
, combining (
9.42
) and (
9.37
) we can find a
C_{5}=C_{5}(n,s_{0},s,\mu_{0},M_{0},M_{1})>0
such that

\|\Phi_{1}\|_{\mathscr{C}^{s+1}(\mathbb{B}^{n};\mathbb{R}^{n})}+\|\Phi_{1}^{*}\theta\|_{\mathscr{C}^{s+1}(\mathbb{B}^{n};\mathbb{M}^{n\times n})}\leq C_{5}(n,s_{0},s,\mu_{0},M_{0},M_{1}).

(9.43)

Applying
[
Str21
, Lemma 5.7]
on (
9.43
), (
9.38
) and (
9.39
) we see that
\|I+\widehat{A}\|_{\mathscr{C}^{s+1}}\lesssim_{n,s_{0},s,\mu_{0},M_{0},M_{1}}1
. So there is a
C_{6}=C_{6}(n,s_{0},s,\mu_{0},M_{0},M_{1})
such that

\|\widehat{A}\|_{\mathscr{C}^{s+1}(\mathbb{B}^{n};\mathbb{M}^{n\times n})}\leq C_{6}(n,s_{0},s,\mu_{0},M_{0},M_{1}).

(9.44)

Take
K_{1}=\max(C_{5},C_{6})
, we get (
9.11
) which completes the proof of
(ii)
.
∎

Taking
\Phi=\Phi_{1}\circ\Phi_{0}
nearly completes the proof of Theorem
9.1
(see also
[
Str21
, Theorem 2.14]
) except we have not established
the injectivity of
\Phi
, since we have only shown
\Phi_{0}
is a local
C^{1}
-diffeomorphism rather than a global
C^{1}
-diffeomorphism onto its image. This problem can be resolved through the next result:

Lemma 9.8
.

Let
s_{0},\mu_{0},M_{0}>0
be as in Proposition
9.4
. Then there is a
\mu_{1}=\mu_{1}(n,s_{0},\mu_{0},M_{0})\in(0,1]
depending only on
n,s_{0},\mu_{0},M_{0}
and satisfying the following:

If
C^{1}
-vector fields
X_{1},\dots,X_{n}
on
\mathfrak{M}
that satisfy the assumptions of Proposition
9.4
with addition that
9
9

9

Also see the quantity
\eta>0
in
[
SS18
, Section 3.2]
.
:

•

Let
U:=\Phi_{0}\circ\Phi_{1}(\mathbb{B}^{n})\subseteq\mathfrak{M}
. For any point
q\in U
and
\mu\in(0,\mu_{0}]
, if the exponential
t\mapsto e^{t\cdot X}(q)
is defined for
t\in B^{n}(0,\mu)
, then
e^{t\cdot X}(q)\neq q
holds for
t\in B^{n}(0,\mu)\backslash\{0\}
.

Then
\Phi_{0}\circ\Phi_{1}
is injective in
B^{n}(0,\mu_{1})
. Moreover
\Phi_{0}\circ\Phi_{1}\big|_{B^{n}(0,\mu_{1})}:B^{n}(0,\mu_{1})\to\mathfrak{M}
is
C^{2}
-diffeomorphism onto its image.

Remark 9.9
.

By Proposition
9.6
,
\Phi_{1}:\mathbb{B}^{n}\to B^{n}(\mu_{0})
is a
\mathscr{C}^{s_{0}+1}
-diffeomorphism onto its image and satisfies
\Phi_{1}(0)=0
.
By (
9.8
), we have
\Phi_{1}^{*}(I+A)\nabla=\widehat{K}(I+\widehat{A})\nabla
where
\|A\|_{C^{0}},\|\widehat{A}\|_{C^{0}}\leq\frac{1}{2}
, so

\|(\nabla\Phi_{1})^{-1}\|_{C^{0}(\mathbb{B}^{n};\mathbb{M}^{n\times n})}=\|\Phi_{1}^{*}\nabla\|_{C^{0}(\mathbb{B}^{n};\mathbb{M}^{n\times n})}\leq\widehat{K}\|I+\widehat{A}\|_{C^{0}(\mathbb{B}^{n};\mathbb{M}^{n\times n})}\|(I+A)^{-1}\|_{C^{0}(\mu_{0}\mathbb{B}^{n};\mathbb{M}^{n\times n})}\leq 3\widehat{K}.

(9.45)

Here
\widehat{K}
is an
\{s_{0}\}
-admissible constant. (
9.45
) implies
\Phi_{1}(B^{n}(0,r))\supseteq B^{n}(0,3\widehat{K}r)
for all
r\in(0,1]
, therefore Lemma
9.8
tells us that
\Phi_{0}\big|_{B^{n}((3\widehat{K})^{-1}\mu_{1})}:B^{n}\left(0,(3\widehat{K})^{-1}\mu_{1}\right)\to\mathfrak{M}
is injective.

Proof.

By Proposition
9.4
, we have
Y=\Phi_{0}^{*}X=(I+A)\frac{\partial}{\partial x}
and
\Phi_{1}^{*}Y=\widehat{K}(I+\widehat{A})\frac{\partial}{\partial t}
are such that
\|A\|_{C^{0}(B^{n}(\mu_{0});\mathbb{M}^{n\times n})},\|\widehat{A}\|_{C^{0}(\mathbb{B}^{n};\mathbb{M}^{n\times n})}\leq\frac{1}{2}
and
\widehat{K}\lesssim_{n,s_{0},\mu_{0},M_{0}}1
(
\widehat{K}
is an
\{s_{0}\}
-admissible constant).

Clearly
\|(I+\widehat{A})^{-1}\|_{C^{0}(\mathbb{B}^{n};\mathbb{M}^{n\times n})}\leq 2
, so

\inf\limits_{t\in\mathbb{B}^{n}}\det(\Phi_{1}^{*}Y)(t)\geq\widehat{K}\|(I+\widehat{A})^{-1}\|_{C^{0}(\mathbb{B}^{n};\mathbb{M}^{n\times n})}^{-n}\geq 2^{-n}\widehat{K}\gtrsim_{n,s_{0},\mu_{0},M_{0}}1.

On the other hand by (
9.9
) we have

\|\Phi_{1}^{*}Y\|_{C^{1}(\mathbb{B}^{n};\mathbb{M}^{n\times n})}\lesssim_{n,s_{0}}\|\Phi_{1}^{*}Y\|_{\mathscr{C}^{s_{0}+1}(\mathbb{B}^{n};\mathbb{M}^{n\times n})}=\widehat{K}\|I+\widehat{A}\|_{\mathscr{C}^{s_{0}+1}(\mathbb{B}^{n};\mathbb{M}^{n\times n})}\leq\widehat{K}(1+K_{0})\lesssim_{n,s_{0},\mu_{0},M_{0}}1.

And by (
9.7
)
we have

\sum_{i,j,k=1}^{n}\|(\Phi_{0}\circ\Phi_{1})^{*}c_{ij}^{k}\|_{C^{0}(\mathbb{B}^{n})}\leq\sum_{i,j,k=1}^{n}\|c_{ij}^{k}\|_{C^{0}(\mathfrak{M})}\leq M_{0}\lesssim_{M_{0}}1.

Therefore applying
[
SS18
, Proposition 9.15]
to the map
\Phi_{0}\circ\Phi_{1}(t)=e^{t\cdot\Phi_{1}^{*}Y}(0)
we can find a
\mu_{1}>0
that depends only on
n,s_{0},\mu_{0}
such that
\Phi_{0}\circ\Phi_{1}
is injective on
B^{n}(\mu_{1})
.

Since
(X_{1},\dots,X_{n})
and
(\Phi_{0}\circ\Phi_{1})^{*}(X_{1},\dots,X_{n})
are both
C^{1}
and span their respective tangent spaces at every point, we know
\Phi_{0}\circ\Phi_{1}|_{B^{n}(0,\mu_{1})}
is a
C^{2}
-map with non-degenerate tangent map at every point in the domain. Since we
have also shown it is injective, we conclude it is a
C^{2}
-diffeomorphism onto its image.
∎

By combining Lemma
9.8
, Propositions
9.4
, and
9.6
, we can prove Theorem
9.1
:

Proof of Theorem
9.1
.

As mentioned before, once we establish
[
Str21
, Theorem 2.14]

for
s,s_{0}>0
, the same follows for
[
Str20b
, Theorem 4.5]
.
Thus, we prove only
[
Str21
, Theorem 2.14]
for
s,s_{0}>0
.

Fix
x_{0}\in\mathfrak{M}
. Since the results
[
Str21
, Theorem 2.14 (a), (b), and (c)]
do not depend on
s_{0}
and
s
, we do not need to change their proof.

Recall, we have reordered
X_{1},\ldots,X_{q}
such that (
9.1
)
holds with
j_{1}^{0}=1,\ldots,j_{n}^{0}=n
.
Set
X_{J_{0}}:=(X_{1},\dots,X_{n})
.
Let
\Phi_{0}(t):=e^{t_{1}X_{1}+\dots+t_{n}X_{n}}(x_{0})
. By Lemma
9.2
we can find a
0
-admissible constant
\mu_{0}
such that
\Phi_{0}:B^{n}(\mu_{0})\to\mathfrak{M}
is a local
C^{1}
-diffeomorphism. And moreover by writing
Y_{i}:=\Phi_{0}^{*}X_{i}
for
i=1,\dots,q
and
Y_{J_{0}}=:(I+A)\nabla
, we have
\|A\|_{C^{0}(B^{n}(\mu_{0});\mathbb{M}^{n\times n})}\leq\frac{1}{2}
,
\|A\|_{\mathscr{C}^{s}(B^{n}(\mu_{0});\mathbb{M}^{n\times n})}\lesssim_{\{s\}}1
. And we can find
(b_{k}^{l})_{1\leq l\leq n<k\leq q}
and
(\tilde{c}_{ij}^{k})_{i,j,k=1}^{n}
such that
Y_{k}=\sum_{l=1}^{n}b_{k}^{l}Y_{l}
,
\Phi_{0}^{*}[X_{i},X_{j}]=\sum_{l=1}^{n}\tilde{c}_{ij}^{l}Y_{l}
for
1\leq i,j\leq n<k\leq q
with
\sum_{l=1}^{n}\sum_{k=n+1}^{q}\|b_{k}^{l}\|_{\mathscr{C}^{s+1}(B^{n}(\mu_{0}))}+\sum_{i,j,l=1}^{n}\|\tilde{c}_{ij}^{l}\|_{\mathscr{C}^{s}(B^{n}(\mu_{0}))}\lesssim_{\{s\}}1
.

Let
\Phi_{1}
be the map given in Proposition
9.4
, and let
\mu_{1}>0
be the constant (which is
\{s_{0}\}
-admissible) from Lemma
9.8
. We define
\Phi:B^{n}(1)\to\mathfrak{M}
by

\Phi(t):=\Phi_{0}\circ\Phi_{1}(\mu_{1}\cdot t).

(9.46)

By Lemma
9.8
,
\Phi
is a
C^{2}
-diffeomorphism onto its image and
[
Str21
, Theorem 2.14 (d), (e) and (g)]
follow.

By (
9.8
) and (
9.46
), (where we use
X=X_{J_{0}}
and
Y=Y_{J_{0}}
in Proposition
9.4
), we have
(\Phi_{0}\circ\Phi_{1})^{*}X_{J_{0}}=\Phi_{1}^{*}Y_{J_{0}}=\widehat{K}(1+\widehat{A})\frac{\partial}{\partial t}
, so

\Phi^{*}X_{J_{0}}(t)=\tfrac{\widehat{K}}{\mu_{1}}(I+\widehat{A}(\mu_{1}\cdot t))\nabla,\quad t\in B^{n}(1).

(9.47)

Note that
\tfrac{\widehat{K}}{\mu_{1}}
is bounded by a
\{s_{0}\}
-admissible constant and by Proposition
9.4

(i)
we have
\widehat{A}(0)=0
and
\|\widehat{A}(\mu_{1}\cdot)\|_{C^{0}(\mathbb{B}^{n};\mathbb{M}^{n\times n})}\leq\frac{1}{2}
and
[
Str21
, Theorem 2.14 (h) and (i)]
follow. In particular we have

\tfrac{\widehat{K}}{2\mu_{1}}\operatorname{dist}_{\Phi^{*}X_{J_{0}}}(t_{1},t_{2})\leq\operatorname{dist}_{\nabla}(t_{1},t_{2})\leq\tfrac{3\widehat{K}}{2\mu_{1}}\operatorname{dist}_{\Phi^{*}X_{J_{0}}}(t_{1},t_{2}),\quad\forall t_{1},t_{2}\in\mathbb{B}^{n}.

Since
|t_{1}-t_{2}|=\operatorname{dist}_{\nabla}(t_{1},t_{2})
, taking pushforward of
\Phi
we get
B_{X_{J_{0}}}(p,\tfrac{2\mu_{1}}{3\widehat{K}})\subseteq\Phi(\mathbb{B}^{n})\subseteq B_{X_{J_{0}}}(p,\tfrac{2\mu_{1}}{\widehat{K}})
.

By Lemma
9.2

(ii)
, we have
Y_{k}=\sum_{l=1}^{n}b_{k}^{l}Y_{l}
for
n+1\leq k\leq q
such that
\|b_{k}^{l}\|_{C^{0}(B^{n}(\mu_{0}))}\lesssim_{s_{0}}\|b_{k}^{l}\|_{\mathscr{C}^{s_{0}+1}(B^{n}(\mu_{0}))}\lesssim_{\{s_{0}\}}1
. So on the set
\Phi_{1}(B^{n}(\mu_{0}))
(note that
\Phi_{0}\big|_{\Phi_{1}(B^{n}(\mu_{0}))}
is injective), there is a
\{s_{0}\}
-admissible constant
C_{1}>0
such that

\operatorname{dist}_{Y_{J_{0}}}(x_{1},x_{2})\leq C_{1}\operatorname{dist}_{Y}(x_{1},x_{2}),\quad\forall x_{1},x_{2}\in\Phi_{1}(B^{n}(\mu_{0})).

Taking pushforward of
\Phi_{0}
we get
B_{X}(p,\tfrac{2\mu_{1}}{3C_{1}\widehat{K}})\subseteq B_{X_{J_{0}}}(p,\tfrac{2\mu_{1}}{3\widehat{K}})
. This proves
[
Str21
, Theorem 2.14 (f)]
.

Combining (
9.47
) and (
9.44
), since
\tfrac{\widehat{K}}{\mu_{1}}
is
\{s_{0}\}
-admissible which is
\{s\}
-admissible, we get
[
Str21
, Theorem 2.14 (j)]
.

Finally the proof of
[
Str21
, Theorem 2.14 (k) and (l)]
is the same as in
[
Str21
, Section 7]
.
∎
